+}
+
+impl HostFacts for LiveHostFacts {
+    fn os_version(&self) -> Option<OsVersion> {
+        self.os_version
+    }
+
+    fn inno_values(&self, key: &PlainName) -> RecordRead<InnoValues> {
+        self.inno
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .get(key.as_str())
+            .cloned()
+            .unwrap_or(RecordRead::Absent)
+    }
+
+    fn pkg_outputs(&self, pkg_id: &PlainName) -> RecordRead<PkgOutputs> {
+        self.pkg
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .get(pkg_id.as_str())
+            .cloned()
+            .unwrap_or(RecordRead::Absent)
+    }
+}
+
+/// What a scripted launch writes as `<job stem>.result.json` beside the job, given the job's path (`None`: nothing).
+pub type ScriptedResult = Box<dyn Fn(&Path) -> Option<Vec<u8>> + Send>;
+
+/// One scripted launch of `FakeElevation` (plan §4.4).
+pub struct Scripted {
+    pub outcome: LaunchOutcome,
+    pub result: Option<ScriptedResult>,
+}
+
+/// Phase 10 (plan §4.4, PD-9): an `Elevation` that never starts a process. Every `launch` reads the job named by
+/// `args[1]` into `jobs`, records the command, then answers with the next scripted launch (writing its result file),
+/// or with `otherwise` when none is left. Its guard is its own, so parallel tests never share one.
+pub struct FakeElevation {
+    pub helper: Option<PathBuf>,
+    pub script: Mutex<VecDeque<Scripted>>,
+    pub launches: Mutex<Vec<(ElevatedCommand, Duration)>>,
+    pub jobs: Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>,
+    /// The outcome of a launch with no scripted entry left.
+    pub otherwise: LaunchOutcome,
+    guard: ElevationGuard,
+}
+
+/// The helper path a `FakeElevation` reports by default: a bare name, never executed.
+const FAKE_HELPER: &str = "fake-krate-manager-helper";
+
+impl FakeElevation {
+    fn with_outcome(helper: Option<PathBuf>, otherwise: LaunchOutcome) -> FakeElevation {
+        FakeElevation {
+            helper,
+            script: Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()),
+            launches: Mutex::new(Vec::new()),
+            jobs: Mutex::new(Vec::new()),
+            otherwise,
+            guard: ElevationGuard::new(),
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Every launch is `Refused`.
+    pub fn refusing() -> FakeElevation {
+        FakeElevation::with_outcome(Some(PathBuf::from(FAKE_HELPER)), LaunchOutcome::Refused)
+    }
+
+    /// Every unscripted launch is `outcome`.
+    pub fn returning(outcome: LaunchOutcome) -> FakeElevation {
+        FakeElevation::with_outcome(Some(PathBuf::from(FAKE_HELPER)), outcome)
+    }
+
+    /// No helper beside the manager (`helper()` is `None`).
+    pub fn missing() -> FakeElevation {
+        FakeElevation::with_outcome(None, LaunchOutcome::Failed("E_ELEVATION_NONE"))
+    }
+
+    pub fn with_helper(self, path: PathBuf) -> FakeElevation {
+        FakeElevation {
+            helper: Some(path),
+            ..self
+        }
+    }
+
+    pub fn push(&self, s: Scripted) {
+        self.script
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .push_back(s);
+    }
+
+    pub fn launches(&self) -> Vec<(ElevatedCommand, Duration)> {
+        self.launches
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .clone()
+    }
+
+    pub fn jobs(&self) -> Vec<Vec<u8>> {
+        self.jobs
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .clone()
+    }
+}
+
+impl Elevation for FakeElevation {
+    fn helper(&self) -> Option<&Path> {
+        self.helper.as_deref()
+    }
+
+    fn launch(&self, command: &ElevatedCommand, timeout: Duration) -> LaunchOutcome {
+        let job = command
+            .args
+            .get(1)
+            .map(PathBuf::from)
+            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("FakeElevation: no job argument in {:?}", command.args));
+        let bytes = std::fs::read(&job)
+            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("FakeElevation cannot read the job {}: {e}", job.display()));
+        self.jobs
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .push(bytes);
+        self.launches
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .push((command.clone(), timeout));
+        let next = self
+            .script
+            .lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .pop_front();
+        let Some(scripted) = next else {
+            return self.otherwise;
+        };
+        if let Some(result) = &scripted.result
+            && let Some(bytes) = result(&job)
+        {
+            let stem = job
+                .file_stem()
+                .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
+                .unwrap_or_default();
+            let path = job.with_file_name(format!("{stem}.result.json"));
+            std::fs::write(&path, bytes)
+                .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("FakeElevation cannot write {}: {e}", path.display()));
+        }
+        scripted.outcome
+    }
+
+    fn guard(&self) -> &ElevationGuard {
+        &self.guard
+    }
+}
+
+/// Phase 10: a `FakeHostIo` shared across threads (the `InstallRig` field); each call locks it.
+impl HostIo for Mutex<FakeHostIo> {
+    fn run(&self, argv: &[&str], bound: Duration) -> RunOutcome {
+        self.lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .run(argv, bound)
+    }
+
+    fn registry_values(&self, subkey: &str, names: [&str; 2]) -> RegistryRead {
+        self.lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .registry_values(subkey, names)
+    }
+
+    fn rtl_version(&self) -> Option<(u32, u32, u32)> {
+        self.lock()
+            .unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+            .rtl_version()
+    }
+}

+/// The Phase 10 scan set: the workspace `.rs` files plus `src-tauri/examples/*.rs` (PD-23).
+fn phase10_files() -> Vec<(String, String)> {
+    let mut files = workspace_rs_files();
+    let examples = app_root().join("src-tauri").join("examples");
+    if examples.is_dir() {
+        files.extend(rs_files(&examples, TAURI_EXAMPLES));
+    }
+    files
+}
+
+/// The token ranges of a file's code that `#[cfg(test)]` or
+/// `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-support"))]` gates (FR-112: "`Seams` built outside `#[cfg(test)]`
+/// code"); the feature literal is blanked in the code text, so it is looked up by byte offset.
+fn test_gated_spans(l: &Lexed, toks: &[&str]) -> Vec<Range<usize>> {
+    let mut spans = cfg_test_spans(toks, &tokens("# [ cfg ( test ) ]"));
+    let any = tokens("# [ cfg ( any ( test , feature = ) ) ]");
+    let offsets = spanned_tokens(&l.code);
+    for (at, span) in seq_at(toks, &any)
+        .into_iter()
+        .zip(cfg_test_spans(toks, &any))
+    {
+        // `=` is token 9 of the attribute, the `)` after the literal token 10.
+        let (from, to) = (offsets[at + 9].0, offsets[at + 10].0);
+        if literal_between(&l.literals, from, to).is_some_and(|lit| lit.text == "test-support") {
+            spans.push(span);
+        }
+    }
+    spans
+}
+
+/// Whether `Name {` at token `at` (the name) declares the type or opens a body after a return type,
+/// rather than building a value.
+fn is_type_brace(toks: &[&str], at: usize) -> bool {
+    // Step back over a path prefix (`os_elevation :: NativeElevation`).
+    let mut k = at;
+    while k >= 3 && toks[k - 1] == ":" && toks[k - 2] == ":" && is_ident(toks[k - 3]) {
+        k -= 3;
+    }
+    let arrow = k >= 2 && toks[k - 1] == ">" && toks[k - 2] == "-";
+    arrow
+        || k.checked_sub(1)
+            .is_some_and(|p| ["struct", "enum", "union", "impl", "for", "trait"].contains(&toks[p]))
+}
+
+/// Phase 10 (plan §4.8.1; FR-024, FR-112, SI-25, SI-29; tasks TA-12) over `phase10_files`
+/// (`injection.rs` exempt: it spells every needle). A "non-test" source is one `is_test_source`
+/// rejects (SI-25).
+/// - The `ELEVATION_WORDS` only in `ELEVATION_HOMES`.
+/// - `HELPER_ONLY_LITERALS` only in the helper's non-test sources and `ops_tests.rs`;
+///   `HELPER_ONLY_FFI` only in the helper's non-test sources.
+/// - `REAL_SEAMS` named only in `os.rs` (their definitions), `main.rs` and `os_tests.rs`; `Seams {`
+///   built in no non-test source but `main.rs`, outside test-gated code.
+/// - `HELPER_CLOCKS` in no non-test helper source but `os.rs`.
+/// - `NativeElevation :: <fn> (` only in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`; `NativeElevation {` built only
+///   in `os_elevation.rs` (its own constructor).
+/// - FR-024: no `FR024_IDENTIFIERS` fragment in a code identifier and no `FR024_LITERALS` word in
+///   a literal of a non-test helper source.
+/// - `killall` (literal or token) only in `platform/mod.rs`.
+fn phase10_findings(files: &[(String, String)]) -> Vec<Finding> {
+    let mut out = Vec::new();
+    for l in lex(files).iter().filter(|l| l.rel != INJECTION_RS) {
+        let rel = l.rel.as_str();
+        let toks = tokens(&l.code);
+        let values: Vec<String> = l.literals.iter().map(literal_value).collect();
+        let test = is_test_source(rel);
+        let helper_code = rel.starts_with(HELPER_SRC) && !test;
+        let in_code = |needle: &str| seq_at(&toks, &tokens(needle)).len();
+        let in_literals = |needle: &str, fold: bool| {
+            let needle_folded = needle.to_lowercase();
+            values
+                .iter()
+                .filter(|v| {
+                    if fold {
+                        v.to_lowercase().contains(&needle_folded)
+                    } else {
+                        v.contains(needle)
+                    }
+                })
+                .count()
+        };
+        let mut report = |rule: &'static str, detail: String| {
+            out.push(Finding {
+                file: rel.to_owned(),
+                rule,
+                detail,
+            });
+        };
+        if !ELEVATION_HOMES.contains(&rel) {
+            for word in ELEVATION_WORDS {
+                let n = in_code(word) + in_literals(word, true);
+                if n > 0 {
+                    report(
+                        RULE_ELEVATION_WORD,
+                        format!(
+                            "`{word}` {n} time(s) (only in {})",
+                            ELEVATION_HOMES.join(", ")
+                        ),
+                    );
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if !helper_code && rel != HELPER_OPS_TESTS_RS {
+            for needle in HELPER_ONLY_LITERALS {
+                let n = in_literals(needle, false);
+                if n > 0 {
+                    report(
+                        RULE_HELPER_ONLY,
+                        format!("{needle:?} {n} time(s) (only in the helper's sources)"),
+                    );
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if !helper_code {
+            for needle in HELPER_ONLY_FFI {
+                let n = in_code(needle);
+                if n > 0 {
+                    report(
+                        RULE_HELPER_ONLY,
+                        format!("`{needle}` {n} time(s) (only in the helper's sources)"),
+                    );
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if !REAL_SEAM_HOMES.contains(&rel) {
+            for name in REAL_SEAMS {
+                let n = in_code(name);
+                if n > 0 {
+                    report(
+                        RULE_REAL_SEAMS,
+                        format!("`{name}` {n} time(s) (built only in main.rs; tests: os_tests.rs)"),
+                    );
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if !test && rel != HELPER_MAIN_RS {
+            let gated = test_gated_spans(l, &toks);
+            let n = seq_at(&toks, &["Seams", "{"])
+                .into_iter()
+                .filter(|&p| !is_type_brace(&toks, p) && !gated.iter().any(|g| g.contains(&p)))
+                .count();
+            if n > 0 {
+                report(
+                    RULE_SEAMS_BUILT,
+                    format!("`Seams {{` {n} time(s) outside test-gated code (only in main.rs)"),
+                );
+            }
+        }
+        if helper_code && rel != HELPER_OS_RS {
+            for clock in HELPER_CLOCKS {
+                let n = in_code(clock);
+                if n > 0 {
+                    report(
+                        RULE_HELPER_CLOCK,
+                        format!("`{clock}` {n} time(s) (only in {HELPER_OS_RS})"),
+                    );
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        let native: Vec<usize> = seq_at(&toks, &["NativeElevation"])
+            .into_iter()
+            .filter(|&p| {
+                let call = toks
+                    .get(p + 1..p + 5)
+                    .is_some_and(|w| w[0] == ":" && w[1] == ":" && is_ident(w[2]) && w[3] == "(");
+                let literal = toks.get(p + 1) == Some(&"{") && !is_type_brace(&toks, p);
+                (call && rel != TAURI_LIB_RS) || (literal && rel != OS_ELEVATION_RS)
+            })
+            .collect();
+        if !native.is_empty() {
+            report(
+                RULE_NATIVE_ELEVATION,
+                format!(
+                    "`NativeElevation` built {} time(s) (only through resolve_elevation in {TAURI_LIB_RS})",
+                    native.len()
+                ),
+            );
+        }
+        if helper_code {
+            for fragment in FR024_IDENTIFIERS {
+                let n = toks
+                    .iter()
+                    .filter(|t| is_ident(t) && t.contains(fragment))
+                    .count();
+                if n > 0 {
+                    report(
+                        RULE_FR024,
+                        format!("an identifier holding `{fragment}` {n} time(s)"),
+                    );
+                }
+            }
+            for word in FR024_LITERALS {
+                let n = in_literals(word, false);
+                if n > 0 {
+                    report(RULE_FR024, format!("{word:?} in {n} literal(s)"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if rel != PLATFORM_MOD_RS {
+            let n = in_code(KILLALL) + in_literals(KILLALL, false);
+            if n > 0 {
+                report(
+                    RULE_KILLALL,
+                    format!("`{KILLALL}` {n} time(s) (only platform::KILLALL_ARGV)"),
+                );
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    out
+}
+

@@ -323,0 +324,237 @@ fn install_identifiers_absent_from_sources() {
+/// FR-113 (plan §4.8.3): the CI fixture's names, the Inno `AppName` and the pkg identifier.
+const FIXTURE_NAME: &str = "Fixture";
+const FIXTURE_PKG_ID: &str = "com.krateaudio.fixture";
+/// The one app directory that may name the fixture.
+const FIXTURE_DIR: &str = "tests/fixtures/";
+/// This guard spells both names; it is not scanned for them.
+const ROSTER_RS: &str = "crates/core/tests/roster.rs";
+/// The manager workflow (repo-relative) and the two elevated jobs that may name the fixture.
+const MANAGER_CI: &str = ".github/workflows/manager-ci.yml";
+const FIXTURE_JOBS: [&str; 2] = ["helper-elevated", "inno-locked-file"];
+/// Directories the app walk never enters, at any depth (build output and dependencies).
+const SKIPPED_DIRS: [&str; 3] = ["target", "node_modules", "dist"];
+
+/// The byte offsets where `word` stands as a whole word in `line` (no ASCII letter, digit or `_`
+/// beside it: `Fixtures` and `FixtureServer` are other words).
+fn word_at(line: &str, word: &str) -> Vec<usize> {
+    let is_word = |b: u8| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b == b'_';
+    let bytes = line.as_bytes();
+    line.match_indices(word)
+        .map(|(at, _)| at)
+        .filter(|&at| {
+            let end = at + word.len();
+            (at == 0 || !is_word(bytes[at - 1])) && bytes.get(end).is_none_or(|&b| !is_word(b))
+        })
+        .collect()
+}
+
+/// Whether byte `at` of a Rust source line lies inside a `"…"` string or a `//` comment: there the
+/// word is text (the fixture's name); elsewhere it is code, a Rust identifier such as
+/// `detect_tests.rs`'s `Input::Fixture` (a heuristic per line, enough for a planted name).
+fn is_rust_text(line: &str, at: usize) -> bool {
+    let bytes = line.as_bytes();
+    let mut quoted = false;
+    let mut i = 0;
+    while i < at {
+        match bytes[i] {
+            b'\\' if quoted => i += 1,
+            b'"' => quoted = !quoted,
+            b'/' if !quoted && bytes.get(i + 1) == Some(&b'/') => return true,
+            _ => {}
+        }
+        i += 1;
+    }
+    quoted
+}
+
+/// The fixture-name hits of one text line as `"<rel>:<line>: <needle>"`: the pkg identifier
+/// anywhere, the name as a whole word (in a Rust source only as text, `is_rust_text`).
+fn fixture_line_hits(rel: &str, number: usize, line: &str, out: &mut Vec<String>) {
+    if line.contains(FIXTURE_PKG_ID) {
+        out.push(format!("{rel}:{number}: {FIXTURE_PKG_ID}"));
+    }
+    let rust = rel.ends_with(".rs");
+    if word_at(line, FIXTURE_NAME)
+        .into_iter()
+        .any(|at| !rust || is_rust_text(line, at))
+    {
+        out.push(format!("{rel}:{number}: {FIXTURE_NAME}"));
+    }
+}
+
+/// Every `"<rel>:<line>: <needle>"` fixture-name hit outside `FIXTURE_DIR` of `app_files`, then
+/// outside the `FIXTURE_JOBS` of `workflow` (FR-113), comments included, case-sensitively.
+fn fixture_name_hits(app_files: &[(String, String)], workflow: &str) -> Vec<String> {
+    let mut hits = Vec::new();
+    for (rel, text) in app_files
+        .iter()
+        .filter(|(r, _)| !r.starts_with(FIXTURE_DIR) && r != ROSTER_RS)
+    {
+        for (index, line) in text.lines().enumerate() {
+            fixture_line_hits(rel, index + 1, line, &mut hits);
+        }
+    }
+    // A top-level key ends `jobs:`; a key indented by exactly two spaces under it names a job.
+    let (mut in_jobs, mut job) = (false, None::<&str>);
+    for (index, line) in workflow.lines().enumerate() {
+        if !line.starts_with([' ', '#']) && !line.trim().is_empty() {
+            in_jobs = line.trim_end() == "jobs:";
+            job = None;
+        } else if in_jobs
+            && let Some(key) = line
+                .strip_prefix("  ")
+                .filter(|rest| !rest.starts_with([' ', '#']))
+                .and_then(|rest| rest.split_once(':'))
+                .map(|(key, _)| key)
+        {
+            job = Some(key);
+        }
+        if !job.is_some_and(|j| FIXTURE_JOBS.contains(&j)) {
+            fixture_line_hits(MANAGER_CI, index + 1, line, &mut hits);
+        }
+    }
+    hits
+}
+
+/// Every file under the app root as (`/`-separated relative path, text read lossily); links are
+/// not followed and no `SKIPPED_DIRS` directory is entered.
+fn app_files() -> Vec<(String, String)> {
+    fn walk(dir: &Path, prefix: &str, out: &mut Vec<(String, String)>) {
+        let entries =
+            fs::read_dir(dir).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("cannot list {}: {e}", dir.display()));
+        for entry in entries {
+            let entry = entry.unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("cannot list {}: {e}", dir.display()));
+            let name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
+            let kind = entry
+                .file_type()
+                .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("cannot stat {}: {e}", entry.path().display()));
+            if kind.is_dir() {
+                if !SKIPPED_DIRS.contains(&name.as_str()) {
+                    walk(&entry.path(), &format!("{prefix}{name}/"), out);
+                }
+            } else if kind.is_file() {
+                match fs::read(entry.path()) {
+                    Ok(bytes) => out.push((
+                        format!("{prefix}{name}"),
+                        String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes).into_owned(),
+                    )),
+                    // A file another writer removed between the listing and the read.
+                    Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {}
+                    Err(e) => panic!("cannot read {}: {e}", entry.path().display()),
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    let mut out = Vec::new();
+    walk(&app_root(), "", &mut out);
+    out.sort();
+    out
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn fixture_names_confined() {
+    let files = app_files();
+    for must in [
+        "crates/core/src/lib.rs",
+        "crates/helper/src/main.rs",
+        "src-tauri/src/lib.rs",
+        "src/main.ts",
+        "package.json",
+        "index.html",
+    ] {
+        assert!(
+            files.iter().any(|(r, _)| r == must),
+            "{must} was not scanned"
+        );
+    }
+    let workflow_path = MANAGER_CI.split('/').fold(repo_root(), |p, c| p.join(c));
+    let workflow = fs::read_to_string(&workflow_path)
+        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("cannot read {}: {e}", workflow_path.display()));
+    assert!(
+        workflow.lines().any(|l| l.trim_end() == "jobs:"),
+        "{MANAGER_CI} has no `jobs:` line: the job scan is vacuous"
+    );
+    let base = fixture_name_hits(&files, &workflow);
+
+    // The task's plant: the fixture's name in `scripts/bundle.js` text, and each name elsewhere.
+    let with = |rel: &str, text: &str| {
+        let mut out: Vec<(String, String)> =
+            files.iter().filter(|(r, _)| r != rel).cloned().collect();
+        let old = files
+            .iter()
+            .find(|(r, _)| r == rel)
+            .map_or(String::new(), |(_, t)| format!("{t}\n"));
+        out.push((rel.to_owned(), format!("{old}{text}\n")));
+        out
+    };
+    let plants = [
+        ("scripts/bundle.js", "const APP = 'Fixture';", FIXTURE_NAME),
+        (
+            "crates/helper/src/ops.rs",
+            "const P: &str = \"com.krateaudio.fixture\";",
+            FIXTURE_PKG_ID,
+        ),
+        (
+            "crates/core/src/install_tests.rs",
+            "const N: &str = \"Fixture\";",
+            FIXTURE_NAME,
+        ),
+        (
+            "crates/helper/src/job_tests.rs",
+            "// the Inno Fixture setup",
+            FIXTURE_NAME,
+        ),
+        ("src/status.ts", "// Fixture", FIXTURE_NAME),
+    ];
+    for (rel, text, needle) in plants {
+        let planted = fixture_name_hits(&with(rel, text), &workflow);
+        let new: Vec<&String> = planted.iter().filter(|h| !base.contains(h)).collect();
+        assert!(
+            new.iter()
+                .any(|h| h.starts_with(&format!("{rel}:")) && h.ends_with(&format!(": {needle}"))),
+            "{rel}: planted {text:?} not reported as {needle}; new hits: {new:?}"
+        );
+    }
+    // A workflow line outside the two jobs is a hit; inside them it is not.
+    let workflow_plant = "jobs:\n  check:\n    steps:\n      - run: echo com.krateaudio.fixture\n  \
+        helper-elevated:\n    steps:\n      - run: echo com.krateaudio.fixture # Fixture\n  \
+        inno-locked-file:\n    steps:\n      - run: echo Fixture\n  after:\n    steps:\n      - run: echo Fixture\n";
+    assert_eq!(
+        fixture_name_hits(&[], workflow_plant),
+        [
+            format!("{MANAGER_CI}:4: {FIXTURE_PKG_ID}"),
+            format!("{MANAGER_CI}:13: {FIXTURE_NAME}"),
+        ],
+        "only the `check` and `after` lines are hits"
+    );
+    // The fixture directory, a Rust identifier `Fixture`, other words and lower case are not hits.
+    for (rel, text) in [
+        (
+            "tests/fixtures/inno/fixture.iss",
+            "#define AppName \"Fixture\"\nAppId=com.krateaudio.fixture",
+        ),
+        (
+            "crates/core/src/detect_tests.rs",
+            "enum Input { Fixture(&'static str) }\nstruct Fixture { base: u8 }\nfn f(x: &Fixture) -> Fixture { Fixture { base: 1 } }",
+        ),
+        (
+            "scripts/bundle.js",
+            "const a = 'Fixtures'; const b = 'FixtureServer'; const c = 'fixture';",
+        ),
+    ] {
+        let planted = fixture_name_hits(&with(rel, text), &workflow);
+        let new: Vec<&String> = planted.iter().filter(|h| !base.contains(h)).collect();
+        assert!(new.is_empty(), "{rel}: quiet {text:?}: {new:?}");
+    }
+    assert!(
+        base.is_empty(),
+        "fixture names outside {FIXTURE_DIR} and the jobs {FIXTURE_JOBS:?} of {MANAGER_CI} ({} app files scanned):\n{}",
+        files.len(),
+        base.join("\n")
+    );
+    println!(
+        "{} app files and {MANAGER_CI} scanned for the fixture names, 0 hits",
+        files.len()
+    );
+}

+//! Phase 10 SC-015 (FR-091; plan §7.1): `install::windows_parameters` checked by the real Windows argument parser
+//! instead of a hand-written one. The quoted string goes onto the command line of the never-shipped `argv-echo`
+//! target through `CommandExt::raw_arg` ("without any quoting or escaping", so the child's runtime parses it as
+//! `ShellExecuteExW`'s `lpParameters` would be), and the argv it echoes must equal the input. Lane `cargo.windows`;
+//! the one process spawned is that echo binary, unelevated; no path below is ever opened.
+#![cfg(windows)]
+
+use krate_manager_core::install::windows_parameters;
+use std::os::windows::process::CommandExt;
+use std::process::Command;
+
+/// A `--job-sha256` value: 64 lower-case hex digits (FR-005).
+const JOB_SHA256: &str = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef";
+
+/// The helper's four arguments (FR-076) around one `--job` path.
+fn helper_args(job: &str) -> Vec<String> {
+    ["--job", job, "--job-sha256", JOB_SHA256]
+        .into_iter()
+        .map(str::to_owned)
+        .collect()
+}
+
+/// The five SC-015 inputs: a path with a space, its verbatim `\\?\` form (FR-027), a quoted path ending in `\` and
+/// in `\\` (the trailing backslashes the MS C rules double before the closing quote), and a path with a tab.
+fn cases() -> Vec<Vec<String>> {
+    let tabbed = [r"C:\Users\John", "\t", r"Smith\job-0123abcd.json"].concat();
+    [
+        r"C:\Users\John Smith\AppData\Local\com.krateaudio.krate-manager\jobs\job-0123abcd.json",
+        r"\\?\C:\Users\John Smith\AppData\Local\com.krateaudio.krate-manager\jobs\job-0123abcd.json",
+        r"C:\Users\John Smith\jobs\",
+        r"C:\Users\John Smith\jobs\\",
+        tabbed.as_str(),
+    ]
+    .into_iter()
+    .map(helper_args)
+    .collect()
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn windows_parameters_parse_back_windows() {
+    for args in cases() {
+        let parameters =
+            windows_parameters(&args).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{args:?} refused: {e}"));
+        let output = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_argv-echo"))
+            .raw_arg(&parameters)
+            .output()
+            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("cannot run argv-echo: {e}"));
+        assert!(
+            output.status.success(),
+            "argv-echo {parameters:?}: {:?}, stderr {}",
+            output.status,
+            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
+        );
+        let echoed: Vec<String> = serde_json::from_slice(&output.stdout).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
+            panic!(
+                "argv-echo {parameters:?} printed no JSON array ({e}): {}",
+                String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout)
+            )
+        });
+        assert_eq!(echoed, args, "lpParameters {parameters:?}");
+    }
+}

+//! The Inno outcome rule (plan §4.1.11; D10, FR-062).
+
+/// What one Inno setup run did (D10).
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub enum InnoVerdict {
+    Installed,
+    Locked,
+    Failed,
+}
+
+/// The exit code Inno Setup 6.7.1 returned when a plug-in file was held open (M-1: Manager CI run 41342540379,
+/// `/SUPPRESSMSGBOXES` defaulting the Abort/Retry/Ignore box to Abort).
+pub const LOCKED_EXIT: i32 = 5;
+/// The fixed part of the file-in-use line that run logged (`tests/fixtures/helper/inno-locked/locked_line.txt`).
+pub const LOCKED_LINE: &str = "DeleteFile: The existing file appears to be in use (5).";
+
+/// `(exit_code, every line of the bounded log tail)` → the verdict: `0` → `Installed`; `Locked` only for the recorded
+/// exit code with the recorded line in the log; every other code `Failed` (FR-062: an Inno that logs differently is a
+/// plain failure, never `Locked`).
+pub fn classify_inno(exit_code: i32, log: &[String]) -> InnoVerdict {
+    if exit_code == 0 {
+        InnoVerdict::Installed
+    } else if exit_code == LOCKED_EXIT && log.iter().any(|l| l.contains(LOCKED_LINE)) {
+        InnoVerdict::Locked
+    } else {
+        InnoVerdict::Failed
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+#[path = "classify_tests.rs"]
+mod tests;

+//! `classify::tests` (plan §7.1 SC-016 row): the measured rule (M-1, FR-062) over the recorded fixtures.
+
+use super::*;
+use crate::result;
+use std::fs;
+use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
+
+fn inno_locked(file: &str) -> PathBuf {
+    Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
+        .join("../../tests/fixtures/helper/inno-locked")
+        .join(file)
+}
+
+fn read(file: &str) -> String {
+    fs::read_to_string(inno_locked(file)).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{file}: {e}"))
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn recorded_locked_case() {
+    assert_eq!(
+        LOCKED_LINE,
+        read("locked_line.txt"),
+        "LOCKED_LINE equals locked_line.txt (SI-26)"
+    );
+    let exit: i32 = read("exit_code.txt")
+        .trim()
+        .parse()
+        .expect("exit_code.txt is an integer");
+    assert_eq!(LOCKED_EXIT, exit);
+    let log = result::log_lines(read("log.txt").as_bytes());
+    assert_eq!(
+        classify_inno(exit, &log),
+        InnoVerdict::Locked,
+        "the recorded run is Locked"
+    );
+
+    // The same code without the recorded line is a plain failure; the line with another code too.
+    let without: Vec<String> = log
+        .iter()
+        .filter(|l| !l.contains(LOCKED_LINE))
+        .cloned()
+        .collect();
+    assert_eq!(
+        without.len(),
+        log.len() - 4,
+        "the recorded log holds the line four times"
+    );
+    assert_eq!(classify_inno(exit, &without), InnoVerdict::Failed);
+    for code in (1..=8).chain([99]).filter(|&c| c != LOCKED_EXIT) {
+        assert_eq!(
+            classify_inno(code, &log),
+            InnoVerdict::Failed,
+            "exit {code}"
+        );
+    }
+    assert_eq!(classify_inno(0, &log), InnoVerdict::Installed);
+    assert_eq!(classify_inno(0, &[]), InnoVerdict::Installed);
+
+    // 500 rollback lines after the recorded line: the bounded tail still holds it (SI-28).
+    let mut long = read("log.txt");
+    for n in 0..500 {
+        long.push_str(&format!(
+            "2026-11-01 19:37:25.{n:03}   Rolling back changes, entry {n}.\r\n"
+        ));
+    }
+    assert_eq!(
+        classify_inno(exit, &result::log_lines(long.as_bytes())),
+        InnoVerdict::Locked
+    );
+}

+//! Arguments and the binary's entry (plan §4.1.3; FR-005, FR-050, FR-052, SI-1).
+
+use crate::digest;
+use crate::error::HelperError;
+use crate::roots::AllowedRoots;
+use crate::seams::Seams;
+use crate::{Exit, HelperOs};
+use std::ffi::OsString;
+use std::path::PathBuf;
+
+/// The parsed argv (FR-005).
+#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub struct Args {
+    pub job: PathBuf,
+    pub job_sha256: [u8; 32],
+    #[cfg(feature = "ci-temp-roots")]
+    pub ci_roots: Option<PathBuf>,
+}
+
+/// The one usage line (exit 2).
+pub const USAGE: &str = "usage: krate-manager-helper --job <path> --job-sha256 <64 lower-case hex>";
+
+/// Store `value` in an empty slot; a repeated flag is a usage error (FR-005).
+fn once<T>(slot: &mut Option<T>, value: T) -> Option<()> {
+    if slot.is_some() {
+        return None;
+    }
+    *slot = Some(value);
+    Some(())
+}
+
+/// The argv without argv[0]; `None` is a usage error (FR-005, PD-25): exactly `--job <path>` and `--job-sha256 <64
+/// lower-case hex>` in either order (plus `--ci-roots <path>` only in a `ci-temp-roots` build), each once, nothing
+/// else.
+pub fn parse_args(args: &[OsString]) -> Option<Args> {
+    let mut job: Option<PathBuf> = None;
+    let mut job_sha256: Option<[u8; 32]> = None;
+    #[cfg(feature = "ci-temp-roots")]
+    let mut ci_roots: Option<PathBuf> = None;
+    let mut rest = args.iter();
+    while let Some(flag) = rest.next() {
+        let value = rest.next()?;
+        match flag.to_str()? {
+            "--job" => once(&mut job, PathBuf::from(value))?,
+            "--job-sha256" => once(&mut job_sha256, digest::parse_hex64(value.to_str()?)?)?,
+            #[cfg(feature = "ci-temp-roots")]
+            "--ci-roots" => once(&mut ci_roots, PathBuf::from(value))?,
+            _ => return None,
+        }
+    }
+    Some(Args {
+        job: job?,
+        job_sha256: job_sha256?,
+        #[cfg(feature = "ci-temp-roots")]
+        ci_roots,
+    })
+}
+
+/// The library entry `main_with` calls (FR-050, SI-17).
+pub type RunFn = fn(&Args, HelperOs, &AllowedRoots, &Seams<'_>) -> Exit;
+
+/// The roots of this run: production, or in a `ci-temp-roots` build given `--ci-roots` the file's (FR-052).
+#[cfg(feature = "ci-temp-roots")]
+fn roots_for(args: &Args, seams: &Seams<'_>) -> Result<AllowedRoots, HelperError> {
+    match &args.ci_roots {
+        Some(file) => crate::roots::from_ci_file(seams.io, file),
+        None => AllowedRoots::production(),
+    }
+}
+
+/// The roots of this run: always production (FR-051).
+#[cfg(not(feature = "ci-temp-roots"))]
+fn roots_for(_args: &Args, _seams: &Seams<'_>) -> Result<AllowedRoots, HelperError> {
+    AllowedRoots::production()
+}
+
+/// Parse, build the roots, take `HelperOs::current()` and call `run` (FR-050). A usage error prints `USAGE` once on
+/// stderr and exits 2 (D15); a root that cannot be built exits 4 `E_HELPER_ROOTS` with nothing written.
+pub fn main_with(args: Vec<OsString>, seams: &Seams<'_>, run: RunFn) -> Exit {
+    let Some(args) = parse_args(&args) else {
+        eprintln!("{USAGE}");
+        return Exit {
+            status: 2,
+            code: None,
+        };
+    };
+    match roots_for(&args, seams) {
+        Ok(roots) => run(&args, HelperOs::current(), &roots, seams),
+        Err(e) => Exit {
+            status: 4,
+            code: Some(e.code),
+        },
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+#[path = "cli_tests.rs"]
+mod tests;

+//! `cli::tests` (owner T004; plan §7.1): FR-005's argument rules, PD-25's malformed hash, SC-019's `--ci-roots` rows.
+
+use super::*;
+use crate::classify::InnoVerdict;
+use crate::digest;
+use crate::seams::fake::{FakeRegistry, ScriptedRunner, TempIo, World};
+use std::cell::RefCell;
+use std::fs;
+use std::path::Path;
+
+thread_local! {
+    /// What the recording `RunFn` was handed, per test thread (no shared lock, Rule 6).
+    static SEEN: RefCell<Vec<(Args, HelperOs, AllowedRoots)>> = const { RefCell::new(Vec::new()) };
+}
+
+fn recording(args: &Args, os: HelperOs, roots: &AllowedRoots, _seams: &Seams<'_>) -> Exit {
+    SEEN.with(|seen| seen.borrow_mut().push((args.clone(), os, roots.clone())));
+    RECORDED
+}
+
+/// What the recording `RunFn` returns.
+const RECORDED: Exit = Exit {
+    status: 0,
+    code: None,
+};
+
+/// FR-005, D15: a usage error.
+const USAGE_EXIT: Exit = Exit {
+    status: 2,
+    code: None,
+};
+
+fn take_seen() -> Vec<(Args, HelperOs, AllowedRoots)> {
+    SEEN.with(|seen| seen.borrow_mut().drain(..).collect())
+}
+
+fn never_classified(_exit_code: i32, _log: &[String]) -> InnoVerdict {
+    InnoVerdict::Failed
+}
+
+/// `main_with` over the fake seams sandboxed to `base`, with the recording `RunFn`.
+fn drive(base: &Path, args: &[OsString]) -> Exit {
+    let world = World::new();
+    let io = TempIo::new(&world, base);
+    let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+    let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+    let seams = Seams {
+        io: &io,
+        runner: &runner,
+        registry: &registry,
+        classify: never_classified,
+    };
+    main_with(args.to_vec(), &seams, recording)
+}
+
+fn argv(parts: &[&str]) -> Vec<OsString> {
+    parts.iter().map(OsString::from).collect()
+}
+
+const HEX: &str = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef";
+
+fn entries(dir: &Path) -> Vec<OsString> {
+    fs::read_dir(dir)
+        .expect("the temp dir lists")
+        .map(|e| e.expect("an entry").file_name())
+        .collect()
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn parse_args_accepts_exactly_the_two_flags() {
+    let digest = digest::parse_hex64(HEX).expect("the fixture hash parses");
+    for parts in [
+        argv(&["--job", "j.json", "--job-sha256", HEX]),
+        argv(&["--job-sha256", HEX, "--job", "j.json"]),
+    ] {
+        let args = parse_args(&parts).expect("either order parses (FR-005)");
+        assert_eq!(args.job, PathBuf::from("j.json"));
+        assert_eq!(args.job_sha256, digest);
+    }
+    let refused: [&[&str]; 9] = [
+        &[],
+        &["--anything"],
+        &["--job", "j.json"],
+        &["--job-sha256", HEX],
+        &["--job", "a", "--job", "b", "--job-sha256", HEX],
+        &["--job", "a", "--job-sha256", HEX, "--job-sha256", HEX],
+        &["--job", "a", "--job-sha256", HEX, "extra"],
+        &["--job", "a", "--job-sha256"],
+        &["--job=a", "--job-sha256", HEX],
+    ];
+    for parts in refused {
+        assert!(
+            parse_args(&argv(parts)).is_none(),
+            "{parts:?} is a usage error"
+        );
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(not(feature = "ci-temp-roots"))]
+#[test]
+fn ci_roots_flag_is_usage_error() {
+    let tmp = tempfile::TempDir::new().expect("a temp dir");
+    let cases: [&[&str]; 3] = [
+        &["--ci-roots", "x"],
+        &["--job", "j.json", "--job-sha256", HEX, "--ci-roots", "x"],
+        &["--ci-roots", "x", "--job-sha256", HEX, "--job", "j.json"],
+    ];
+    for parts in cases {
+        assert!(
+            parse_args(&argv(parts)).is_none(),
+            "{parts:?} parses as None"
+        );
+        assert_eq!(drive(tmp.path(), &argv(parts)), USAGE_EXIT, "{parts:?}");
+    }
+    assert!(take_seen().is_empty(), "the RunFn is never called");
+    assert!(entries(tmp.path()).is_empty(), "nothing written");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn malformed_hash_is_usage_error() {
+    let tmp = tempfile::TempDir::new().expect("a temp dir");
+    let job = tmp.path().join("job.json");
+    let job = job.to_str().expect("a UTF-8 temp path");
+    let upper = HEX.to_ascii_uppercase();
+    for hash in ["xyz", &HEX[..63], upper.as_str()] {
+        let parts = argv(&["--job", job, "--job-sha256", hash]);
+        assert!(parse_args(&parts).is_none(), "{hash:?} is not 64 lower hex");
+        assert_eq!(drive(tmp.path(), &parts), USAGE_EXIT, "{hash:?}");
+    }
+    assert!(take_seen().is_empty(), "the RunFn is never called");
+    assert!(entries(tmp.path()).is_empty(), "nothing written");
+}

+#[cfg(feature = "ci-temp-roots")]
+#[test]
+fn ci_roots_file_is_read() {
+    use crate::error;
+    use crate::roots::{OsRoots, STATE_DIR_NAME, StateDirRule};
+    use std::path::MAIN_SEPARATOR;
+
+    let tmp = tempfile::TempDir::new().expect("a temp dir");
+    let real = tmp.path().join("real");
+    let outside = tmp.path().join("outside");
+    fs::create_dir_all(&real).expect("real/");
+    fs::create_dir_all(&outside).expect("outside/");
+    let state_dir = real.join(STATE_DIR_NAME);
+    let job = state_dir
+        .join("jobs")
+        .join("job-0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef.json");
+    let file = real.join("ci-roots.json");
+    let production = AllowedRoots::production().expect("the production roots resolve");
+    let s = |p: &Path| p.to_str().expect("a UTF-8 temp path").to_owned();
+
+    // The file's keys per OS (FR-052) and the roots they must yield: the plug-in, preset and application roots and
+    // the state dir from the file; `run_base`, `run_ancestors` and `volume` stay production (PD-5).
+    let (keys, expected_os): (Vec<(&str, PathBuf)>, OsRoots) = match &production.os {
+        OsRoots::Windows(w) => {
+            let keys = vec![
+                ("vst3_vendor", real.join("vst3").join("Krate Audio")),
+                ("preset_vendor", real.join("presets")),
+            ];
+            let mut w = w.clone();
+            w.vst3_vendor = keys[0].1.clone();
+            w.preset_vendor = keys[1].1.clone();
+            (keys, OsRoots::Windows(w))
+        }
+        OsRoots::Macos(m) => {
+            let keys = vec![
+                ("vst3", real.join("VST3")),
+                ("components", real.join("Components")),
+                ("presets", real.join("presets")),
+                ("applications", real.join("Applications")),
+            ];
+            let mut m = m.clone();
+            m.vst3 = keys[0].1.clone();
+            m.components = keys[1].1.clone();
+            m.presets = keys[2].1.clone();
+            m.applications = keys[3].1.clone();
+            (keys, OsRoots::Macos(m))
+        }
+        OsRoots::None => (Vec::new(), OsRoots::None),
+    };
+    let write = |pairs: &[(&str, String)]| {
+        let mut object = serde_json::Map::new();
+        for (k, v) in pairs {
+            object.insert((*k).to_owned(), serde_json::Value::String(v.clone()));
+        }
+        fs::write(&file, serde_json::to_vec(&object).expect("JSON")).expect("ci-roots.json");
+    };
+    let mut good: Vec<(&str, String)> = vec![("state_dir", s(&state_dir))];
+    good.extend(keys.iter().map(|(k, p)| (*k, s(p))));
+    let parts = argv(&[
+        "--ci-roots",
+        &s(&file),
+        "--job",
+        &s(&job),
+        "--job-sha256",
+        HEX,
+    ]);
+
+    write(&good);
+    assert_eq!(drive(tmp.path(), &parts), RECORDED);
+    let seen = take_seen();
+    assert_eq!(seen.len(), 1, "the RunFn is called once");
+    let (args, helper_os, roots) = &seen[0];
+    assert_eq!(args.ci_roots.as_deref(), Some(file.as_path()));
+    assert_eq!(args.job, job);
+    assert_eq!(*helper_os, HelperOs::current());
+    assert_eq!(
+        *roots,
+        AllowedRoots {
+            os: expected_os,
+            state_dir: StateDirRule::Exact(state_dir.clone()),
+        }
+    );
+
+    // A repeated `--ci-roots` is a usage error (FR-005).
+    let mut repeated = parts.clone();
+    repeated.extend(argv(&["--ci-roots", &s(&file)]));
+    assert_eq!(drive(tmp.path(), &repeated), USAGE_EXIT);
+
+    // Refused, exit 4 `E_HELPER_ROOTS`, the RunFn never called: a root (or the state dir) outside the file's
+    // directory, a `..` escape, a relative path, a missing key, a key `--ci-roots` may not set (PD-5), not JSON.
+    let roots_exit = Exit {
+        status: 4,
+        code: Some(error::ROOTS.code),
+    };
+    let mut bad: Vec<Vec<(&str, String)>> = Vec::new();
+    for i in 0..good.len() {
+        let mut out_of_bounds = good.clone();
+        out_of_bounds[i].1 = s(&outside.join("x"));
+        bad.push(out_of_bounds);
+        let mut escape = good.clone();
+        escape[i].1 = format!(
+            "{}{MAIN_SEPARATOR}..{MAIN_SEPARATOR}..{MAIN_SEPARATOR}outside",
+            good[i].1
+        );
+        bad.push(escape);
+        let mut relative = good.clone();
+        relative[i].1 = "relative".to_owned();
+        bad.push(relative);
+        let mut missing = good.clone();
+        missing.remove(i);
+        bad.push(missing);
+    }
+    let mut extra = good.clone();
+    extra.push(("run_base", s(&real.join("run"))));
+    bad.push(extra);
+    for pairs in &bad {
+        write(pairs);
+        assert_eq!(drive(tmp.path(), &parts), roots_exit, "{pairs:?}");
+    }
+    fs::write(&file, b"not json").expect("ci-roots.json");
+    assert_eq!(drive(tmp.path(), &parts), roots_exit, "not JSON");
+    assert!(take_seen().is_empty(), "no refused file reaches the RunFn");
+}

+//! SHA-256 and hex (plan §4.1.8): the helper's only `sha2` user (SI-6).
+
+use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
+
+/// A streaming SHA-256 (FR-035's hash-while-copying).
+pub struct Sha256Stream(Sha256);
+
+impl Sha256Stream {
+    pub fn new() -> Sha256Stream {
+        Sha256Stream(Sha256::new())
+    }
+
+    pub fn update(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) {
+        self.0.update(bytes);
+    }
+
+    pub fn finish(self) -> [u8; 32] {
+        self.0.finalize().into()
+    }
+}
+
+impl Default for Sha256Stream {
+    fn default() -> Sha256Stream {
+        Sha256Stream::new()
+    }
+}
+
+/// The SHA-256 of `bytes`.
+pub fn sha256(bytes: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32] {
+    let mut stream = Sha256Stream::new();
+    stream.update(bytes);
+    stream.finish()
+}
+
+const HEX: &[u8; 16] = b"0123456789abcdef";
+
+/// Lower-case hex, two digits per byte.
+pub fn hex(digest: &[u8; 32]) -> String {
+    let mut s = String::with_capacity(64);
+    for b in digest {
+        s.push(char::from(HEX[usize::from(b >> 4)]));
+        s.push(char::from(HEX[usize::from(b & 0x0f)]));
+    }
+    s
+}
+
+/// The value of one lower-case hex digit.
+fn nibble(c: u8) -> Option<u8> {
+    match c {
+        b'0'..=b'9' => Some(c - b'0'),
+        b'a'..=b'f' => Some(c - b'a' + 10),
+        _ => None,
+    }
+}
+
+/// Exactly 64 lower-case hex digits, else `None` (FR-005, PD-25).
+pub fn parse_hex64(s: &str) -> Option<[u8; 32]> {
+    let bytes = s.as_bytes();
+    if bytes.len() != 64 {
+        return None;
+    }
+    let mut out = [0u8; 32];
+    for (i, pair) in bytes.as_chunks::<2>().0.iter().enumerate() {
+        out[i] = (nibble(pair[0])? << 4) | nibble(pair[1])?;
+    }
+    Some(out)
+}

+//! The job codec (plan §4.1.4; FR-001, FR-002, FR-036; PD-2).
+
+use crate::HelperOs;
+use crate::digest;
+use crate::error::{self, HelperError};
+use serde::{Deserialize, Deserializer};
+
+/// FR-023: the job read bound.
+pub const MAX_JOB_BYTES: u64 = 64 * 1024;
+/// FR-001: at most this many ops per job.
+pub const MAX_OPS: usize = 32;
+
+/// One op of the fixed set (FR-030 to FR-036).
+#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub enum Op {
+    RunInnoInstaller { file: String, sha256: [u8; 32] },
+    RunInnoUninstaller { app_id: String },
+    RunPkgInstaller { file: String, sha256: [u8; 32] },
+    UninstallPkg { pkg_id: String },
+}
+
+impl Op {
+    /// The op's `op` value (FR-001).
+    pub fn kind(&self) -> &'static str {
+        match self {
+            Op::RunInnoInstaller { .. } => "run_inno_installer",
+            Op::RunInnoUninstaller { .. } => "run_inno_uninstaller",
+            Op::RunPkgInstaller { .. } => "run_pkg_installer",
+            Op::UninstallPkg { .. } => "uninstall_pkg",
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// The OS the op runs on (FR-036): Inno ops Windows, pkg ops macOS.
+    pub fn target(&self) -> HelperOs {
+        match self {
+            Op::RunInnoInstaller { .. } | Op::RunInnoUninstaller { .. } => HelperOs::Windows,
+            Op::RunPkgInstaller { .. } | Op::UninstallPkg { .. } => HelperOs::Macos,
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// FR-001's key set per kind, for `job::tests::no_field_adds_a_root`.
+pub const KEYS: [(&str, &[&str]); 4] = [
+    ("run_inno_installer", &["op", "file", "sha256"]),
+    ("run_inno_uninstaller", &["op", "app_id"]),
+    ("run_pkg_installer", &["op", "file", "sha256"]),
+    ("uninstall_pkg", &["op", "pkg_id"]),
+];
+
+/// The job as read (PD-2): no other key, none twice (serde_derive's `duplicate_field`).
+#[derive(Deserialize)]
+#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
+struct RawJob {
+    job_version: u64,
+    ops: Vec<RawOp>,
+}
+
+/// One op as read (PD-2): every key any kind knows, each a `Slot`, so an explicit `null` stays visible.
+#[derive(Deserialize)]
+#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
+struct RawOp {
+    op: String,
+    #[serde(default)]
+    file: Slot,
+    #[serde(default)]
+    sha256: Slot,
+    #[serde(default)]
+    app_id: Slot,
+    #[serde(default)]
+    pkg_id: Slot,
+}
+
+/// A key's value: `Absent` when the key is missing, else `Present`, `null` included (PD-2).
+#[derive(Default)]
+enum Slot {
+    #[default]
+    Absent,
+    Present(serde_json::Value),
+}
+
+impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for Slot {
+    fn deserialize<D: Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Slot, D::Error> {
+        serde_json::Value::deserialize(deserializer).map(Slot::Present)
+    }
+}
+
+impl Slot {
+    /// The string a present key holds; `None` when absent, `null` or another type.
+    fn string(self) -> Option<String> {
+        match self {
+            Slot::Present(serde_json::Value::String(s)) => Some(s),
+            Slot::Absent | Slot::Present(_) => None,
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn is_absent(&self) -> bool {
+        matches!(self, Slot::Absent)
+    }
+}
+
+/// FR-001: the job and each op are JSON objects. serde accepts the array form for a derived struct too
+/// (`serde_json` `deserialize_struct` takes `[`, serde_derive emits `visit_seq`), which would fill the fields by
+/// position, so the shape is checked on a `Value` first; the typed pass then sees every key, repeats included.
+fn objects_only(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<()> {
+    let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_slice(bytes).ok()?;
+    let job = value.as_object()?;
+    if let Some(serde_json::Value::Array(ops)) = job.get("ops")
+        && !ops.iter().all(serde_json::Value::is_object)
+    {
+        return None;
+    }
+    Some(())
+}
+
+/// One op of `kind` with exactly its keys present, each a string (FR-001).
+fn op(raw: RawOp) -> Option<Op> {
+    let RawOp {
+        op: kind,
+        file,
+        sha256,
+        app_id,
+        pkg_id,
+    } = raw;
+    let op = match kind.as_str() {
+        "run_inno_installer" | "run_pkg_installer" => {
+            if !(app_id.is_absent() && pkg_id.is_absent()) {
+                return None;
+            }
+            let file = file.string()?;
+            let sha256 = digest::parse_hex64(&sha256.string()?)?;
+            if kind == "run_inno_installer" {
+                Op::RunInnoInstaller { file, sha256 }
+            } else {
+                Op::RunPkgInstaller { file, sha256 }
+            }
+        }
+        "run_inno_uninstaller" => {
+            if !(file.is_absent() && sha256.is_absent() && pkg_id.is_absent()) {
+                return None;
+            }
+            Op::RunInnoUninstaller {
+                app_id: app_id.string()?,
+            }
+        }
+        "uninstall_pkg" => {
+            if !(file.is_absent() && sha256.is_absent() && app_id.is_absent()) {
+                return None;
+            }
+            Op::UninstallPkg {
+                pkg_id: pkg_id.string()?,
+            }
+        }
+        _ => return None,
+    };
+    Some(op)
+}
+
+/// `parse` with every defect as `None`.
+fn parse_ops(bytes: &[u8], os: HelperOs) -> Option<Vec<Op>> {
+    objects_only(bytes)?;
+    let raw: RawJob = serde_json::from_slice(bytes).ok()?;
+    if raw.job_version != 1 || raw.ops.is_empty() || raw.ops.len() > MAX_OPS {
+        return None;
+    }
+    let ops = raw.ops.into_iter().map(op).collect::<Option<Vec<Op>>>()?;
+    // FR-001, FR-036: every op targets `os`, so `HelperOs::Linux` refuses every job.
+    ops.iter().all(|op| op.target() == os).then_some(ops)
+}
+
+/// The job's ops for `os`, or `E_HELPER_JOB_PARSE` (FR-001, FR-002, FR-036; PD-2). The contents of `file`, `app_id`
+/// and `pkg_id` are checked per op later (FR-020, FR-031, FR-033).
+pub fn parse(bytes: &[u8], os: HelperOs) -> Result<Vec<Op>, HelperError> {
+    parse_ops(bytes, os).ok_or(error::JOB_PARSE)
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+#[path = "job_tests.rs"]
+mod tests;

+//! `job::tests` (owner T003; plan §7.1): FR-001's key sets, FR-002's duplicate keys, FR-036's target OS.
+
+use super::*;
+use crate::error;
+
+const HEX: &str = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef";
+
+/// `{"job_version":1,"ops":[<ops>]}`, spelled by hand so a test can repeat a key.
+fn job(ops: &[String]) -> String {
+    format!("{{\"job_version\":1,\"ops\":[{}]}}", ops.join(","))
+}
+
+fn inno_installer() -> String {
+    format!("{{\"op\":\"run_inno_installer\",\"file\":\"staging/a.exe\",\"sha256\":\"{HEX}\"}}")
+}
+
+fn inno_uninstaller() -> String {
+    "{\"op\":\"run_inno_uninstaller\",\"app_id\":\"{0A1B2C3D-0000-4000-8000-00000000A1FA}\"}"
+        .to_owned()
+}
+
+fn pkg_installer() -> String {
+    format!("{{\"op\":\"run_pkg_installer\",\"file\":\"staging/a.pkg\",\"sha256\":\"{HEX}\"}}")
+}
+
+fn uninstall_pkg() -> String {
+    "{\"op\":\"uninstall_pkg\",\"pkg_id\":\"com.krateaudio.alpha\"}".to_owned()
+}
+
+/// Each kind's valid op text, the OS it targets and the op it parses to.
+fn valid_ops() -> Vec<(String, HelperOs, Op)> {
+    let sha = [
+        0x01, 0x23, 0x45, 0x67, 0x89, 0xab, 0xcd, 0xef, 0x01, 0x23, 0x45, 0x67, 0x89, 0xab, 0xcd,
+        0xef, 0x01, 0x23, 0x45, 0x67, 0x89, 0xab, 0xcd, 0xef, 0x01, 0x23, 0x45, 0x67, 0x89, 0xab,
+        0xcd, 0xef,
+    ];
+    vec![
+        (
+            inno_installer(),
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            Op::RunInnoInstaller {
+                file: "staging/a.exe".to_owned(),
+                sha256: sha,
+            },
+        ),
+        (
+            inno_uninstaller(),
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            Op::RunInnoUninstaller {
+                app_id: "{0A1B2C3D-0000-4000-8000-00000000A1FA}".to_owned(),
+            },
+        ),
+        (
+            pkg_installer(),
+            HelperOs::Macos,
+            Op::RunPkgInstaller {
+                file: "staging/a.pkg".to_owned(),
+                sha256: sha,
+            },
+        ),
+        (
+            uninstall_pkg(),
+            HelperOs::Macos,
+            Op::UninstallPkg {
+                pkg_id: "com.krateaudio.alpha".to_owned(),
+            },
+        ),
+    ]
+}
+
+/// `parse` must refuse `text` for `os` with `E_HELPER_JOB_PARSE`.
+fn refused(text: &str, os: HelperOs, why: &str) {
+    assert_eq!(
+        parse(text.as_bytes(), os),
+        Err(error::JOB_PARSE),
+        "{why}: {text}"
+    );
+}
+
+/// `op_text` with `key: value` added before its closing brace.
+fn with_key(op_text: &str, key: &str, value: &str) -> String {
+    let body = op_text.strip_suffix('}').expect("an op is an object");
+    format!("{body},\"{key}\":{value}}}")
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn no_field_adds_a_root() {
+    // FR-001's four key sets, exactly.
+    let expected: [(&str, &[&str]); 4] = [
+        ("run_inno_installer", &["op", "file", "sha256"]),
+        ("run_inno_uninstaller", &["op", "app_id"]),
+        ("run_pkg_installer", &["op", "file", "sha256"]),
+        ("uninstall_pkg", &["op", "pkg_id"]),
+    ];
+    assert_eq!(KEYS, expected);
+
+    for (text, os, op) in valid_ops() {
+        // The key set parses, and its kind is the one KEYS names.
+        assert_eq!(
+            parse(job(std::slice::from_ref(&text)).as_bytes(), os),
+            Ok(vec![op.clone()])
+        );
+        let keys = KEYS
+            .iter()
+            .find(|(kind, _)| *kind == op.kind())
+            .map(|(_, keys)| *keys)
+            .expect("every kind has a key set");
+
+        // No key that would carry a root, a destination, arguments or a log path is accepted.
+        for (key, value) in [
+            ("roots", "[]"),
+            ("settings_roots", "[\"C:\\\\Windows\"]"),
+            ("dest", "\"/etc\""),
+            ("args", "[\"/DIR=x\"]"),
+            ("log", "\"x.log\""),
+        ] {
+            refused(&job(&[with_key(&text, key, value)]), os, "an extra key");
+        }
+
+        // Every key of the set is required.
+        let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&text).expect("valid op text");
+        for missing in keys {
+            let mut object = value.as_object().expect("an object").clone();
+            object.remove(*missing);
+            let text = serde_json::to_string(&object).expect("serializes");
+            refused(&job(&[text]), os, "a missing key");
+        }
+
+        // Every other kind's keys are refused on this kind.
+        for (_, others) in KEYS {
+            for other in others.iter().filter(|k| !keys.contains(k)) {
+                refused(
+                    &job(&[with_key(&text, other, "\"x\"")]),
+                    os,
+                    "another kind's key",
+                );
+                refused(
+                    &job(&[with_key(&text, other, "null")]),
+                    os,
+                    "another kind's key, null",
+                );
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn parse_refuses_malformed_jobs() {
+    let win = HelperOs::Windows;
+    // PD-2, FR-002: a repeated key, `op` included, is refused, wherever it sits.
+    refused(
+        &job(&[format!(
+            "{{\"op\":\"run_pkg_installer\",\"op\":\"uninstall_pkg\",\"file\":\"a.pkg\",\"sha256\":\"{HEX}\"}}"
+        )]),
+        HelperOs::Macos,
+        "a duplicate op key",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &job(&[format!(
+            "{{\"op\":\"run_inno_installer\",\"file\":\"a.exe\",\"file\":\"b.exe\",\"sha256\":\"{HEX}\"}}"
+        )]),
+        win,
+        "a duplicate file key",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &format!(
+            "{{\"job_version\":1,\"job_version\":1,\"ops\":[{}]}}",
+            inno_installer()
+        ),
+        win,
+        "a duplicate job_version",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &format!(
+            "{{\"job_version\":1,\"ops\":[{}],\"ops\":[{}]}}",
+            inno_installer(),
+            inno_installer()
+        ),
+        win,
+        "a duplicate ops key",
+    );
+
+    // job_version is the integer 1.
+    for version in ["0", "2", "1.0", "\"1\"", "null", "-1"] {
+        refused(
+            &format!(
+                "{{\"job_version\":{version},\"ops\":[{}]}}",
+                inno_installer()
+            ),
+            win,
+            "a job_version other than 1",
+        );
+    }
+    refused(
+        &format!("{{\"ops\":[{}]}}", inno_installer()),
+        win,
+        "no job_version",
+    );
+    refused("{\"job_version\":1}", win, "no ops");
+    refused(
+        &format!(
+            "{{\"job_version\":1,\"ops\":[{}],\"roots\":[]}}",
+            inno_installer()
+        ),
+        win,
+        "an extra top-level key",
+    );
+
+    // 1 to 32 ops.
+    refused(&job(&[]), win, "0 ops");
+    let thirty_three = vec![inno_installer(); 33];
+    refused(&job(&thirty_three), win, "33 ops");
+    let thirty_two = vec![inno_installer(); 32];
+    assert_eq!(
+        parse(job(&thirty_two).as_bytes(), win).map(|ops| ops.len()),
+        Ok(MAX_OPS)
+    );
+
+    // An explicit null is a present key of the wrong type, not an absent one.
+    refused(
+        &job(&["{\"op\":\"run_inno_uninstaller\",\"app_id\":null}".to_owned()]),
+        win,
+        "app_id null",
+    );
+    // Wrong types.
+    refused(
+        &job(&[format!(
+            "{{\"op\":\"run_inno_installer\",\"file\":7,\"sha256\":\"{HEX}\"}}"
+        )]),
+        win,
+        "a number file",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &job(&["{\"op\":\"uninstall_pkg\",\"pkg_id\":[\"com.krateaudio.alpha\"]}".to_owned()]),
+        HelperOs::Macos,
+        "an array pkg_id",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &job(&["{\"op\":7,\"pkg_id\":\"com.krateaudio.alpha\"}".to_owned()]),
+        HelperOs::Macos,
+        "a number op",
+    );
+    // An unknown op value.
+    refused(
+        &job(&["{\"op\":\"run_command\",\"app_id\":\"x\"}".to_owned()]),
+        win,
+        "an unknown op",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &job(&["{\"op\":\"Uninstall_Pkg\",\"pkg_id\":\"com.krateaudio.alpha\"}".to_owned()]),
+        HelperOs::Macos,
+        "an op value in the wrong case",
+    );
+
+    // sha256: 64 lower-case hex only.
+    let upper = HEX.to_ascii_uppercase();
+    refused(
+        &job(&[format!(
+            "{{\"op\":\"run_inno_installer\",\"file\":\"a.exe\",\"sha256\":\"{upper}\"}}"
+        )]),
+        win,
+        "upper-case hex",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &job(&[format!(
+            "{{\"op\":\"run_pkg_installer\",\"file\":\"a.pkg\",\"sha256\":\"{}\"}}",
+            &HEX[1..]
+        )]),
+        HelperOs::Macos,
+        "63 hex digits",
+    );
+
+    // FR-001: a JSON object, not the array form serde would also accept for a struct.
+    refused(
+        &format!("[1,[[\"run_inno_installer\",\"a.exe\",\"{HEX}\"]]]"),
+        win,
+        "the whole job as an array",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &job(&[format!("[\"run_inno_installer\",\"a.exe\",\"{HEX}\"]")]),
+        win,
+        "an op as an array",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &format!("{} {{}}", job(&[inno_installer()])),
+        win,
+        "trailing data",
+    );
+    refused("", win, "empty bytes");
+    refused("not json", win, "not JSON");
+    refused(
+        &format!("\u{feff}{}", job(&[inno_installer()])),
+        win,
+        "a byte-order mark",
+    );
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn parse_checks_the_target_os() {
+    for (text, target, _) in valid_ops() {
+        // FR-036: Linux refuses every op.
+        refused(
+            &job(std::slice::from_ref(&text)),
+            HelperOs::Linux,
+            "an op on Linux",
+        );
+        // FR-001: an op of the other OS refuses the whole job.
+        let other = if target == HelperOs::Windows {
+            HelperOs::Macos
+        } else {
+            HelperOs::Windows
+        };
+        refused(
+            &job(std::slice::from_ref(&text)),
+            other,
+            "an op of the other OS",
+        );
+    }
+    // An Inno op with os = Macos, even beside valid macOS ops.
+    refused(
+        &job(&[pkg_installer(), inno_installer(), uninstall_pkg()]),
+        HelperOs::Macos,
+        "an Inno op in a macOS job",
+    );
+    refused(
+        &job(&[inno_uninstaller(), uninstall_pkg()]),
+        HelperOs::Windows,
+        "a pkg op in a Windows job",
+    );
+    // Both kinds of one OS, in job order.
+    let ops = parse(
+        job(&[inno_uninstaller(), inno_installer(), inno_uninstaller()]).as_bytes(),
+        HelperOs::Windows,
+    )
+    .expect("a Windows job parses");
+    assert_eq!(
+        ops.iter().map(Op::kind).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
+        [
+            "run_inno_uninstaller",
+            "run_inno_installer",
+            "run_inno_uninstaller"
+        ]
+    );
+    // The contents of file, app_id and pkg_id are not checked here (FR-020, FR-031, FR-033 refuse per op).
+    assert_eq!(
+        parse(
+            job(&["{\"op\":\"uninstall_pkg\",\"pkg_id\":\"\"}".to_owned()]).as_bytes(),
+            HelperOs::Macos
+        ),
+        Ok(vec![Op::UninstallPkg {
+            pkg_id: String::new()
+        }])
+    );
+}

-//! krate-manager-helper: the elevated helper, a stub with no operations in this build (D20).
+//! krate-manager-helper: the elevated helper's fixed operation set (Phase 10 plan §4.1, PD-1 to PD-7).
+

+use std::io;
+use std::process::ExitCode;

+/// The OS a job is checked and run for (D18); passed as a value so every OS's logic runs on every leg.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub enum HelperOs {
+    Windows,
+    Macos,
+    Linux,
+}
+
+impl HelperOs {
+    /// The build's own OS: the only cfg-derived OS value (D18); only `cli::main_with` passes it.
+    pub fn current() -> HelperOs {
+        if cfg!(windows) {
+            HelperOs::Windows
+        } else if cfg!(target_os = "macos") {
+            HelperOs::Macos
+        } else {
+            HelperOs::Linux
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// What `run` ended with (PD-4): the process status and the code behind it, if any.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub struct Exit {
+    pub status: u8,
+    pub code: Option<&'static str>,
+}
+
+impl From<Exit> for ExitCode {
+    fn from(exit: Exit) -> ExitCode {
+        ExitCode::from(exit.status)
+    }
+}
+
+/// Exit 4: nothing written (D15).
+fn nothing_written(e: error::HelperError) -> Exit {
+    Exit {
+        status: 4,
+        code: Some(e.code),
+    }
+}
+
+/// Steps 4 to 6 of plan §4.1.2: the bounded read, the hash binding (D2), the parse.
+fn read_job(
+    args: &cli::Args,
+    os: HelperOs,
+    seams: &seams::Seams<'_>,
+) -> Result<Vec<job::Op>, error::HelperError> {
+    let bytes = seams
+        .io
+        .read_bounded(&args.job, job::MAX_JOB_BYTES)
+        .map_err(|_| error::JOB_READ)?;
+    if bytes.len() as u64 > job::MAX_JOB_BYTES {
+        return Err(error::JOB_READ);
+    }
+    if digest::sha256(&bytes) != args.job_sha256 {
+        return Err(error::JOB_HASH);
+    }
+    job::parse(&bytes, os)
+}
+
+/// One job (plan §4.1.2, PD-3): every exit-4 point lies before the result file is reserved, every exit-3 point after
+/// it, so an exit-3 result always exists.
+pub fn run(
+    args: &cli::Args,
+    os: HelperOs,
+    roots: &roots::AllowedRoots,
+    seams: &seams::Seams<'_>,
+) -> Exit {
+    // 1. The roots, canonicalized once (FR-026).
+    let canonical = match roots::canonical(seams.io, roots) {
+        Ok(canonical) => canonical,
+        Err(e) => return nothing_written(e),
+    };
+    // 2. The job's location (FR-021, FR-025, FR-027).
+    let place = match validate::job_location(seams.io, &args.job, &canonical, os) {
+        Ok(place) => place,
+        Err(e) => return nothing_written(e),
+    };
+    // 3. The result file, reserved with `create_new` (FR-003).
+    let mut out = match seams.io.create_new(&place.result_path) {
+        Ok(out) => out,
+        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists => {
+            return nothing_written(error::RESULT_EXISTS);
+        }
+        Err(_) => return nothing_written(error::RESULT_WRITE),
+    };
+    // 4 to 7. Read, hash, parse, run.
+    let (job_error, entries) = match read_job(args, os, seams) {
+        Ok(ops) => {
+            let ctx = ops::OpCtx {
+                os,
+                roots: &canonical,
+                place: &place,
+            };
+            (None, ops::run_batch(&ops, &ctx, seams))
+        }
+        Err(e) => (Some(e.code), Vec::new()),
+    };
+    let file = result::ResultFile {
+        result_version: 1,
+        job_sha256: digest::hex(&args.job_sha256),
+        job_error,
+        ops: entries,
+    };
+    if result::write(&mut *out, &file).is_err() {
+        return nothing_written(error::RESULT_WRITE);
+    }
+    match job_error {
+        Some(code) => Exit {
+            status: 3,
+            code: Some(code),
+        },
+        None => Exit {
+            status: 0,
+            code: None,
+        },
+    }
+}
