+/// SC-016 post-M-1: the recorded `inno-locked/` run (its log and exit code) through the runner and the production
+/// classifier reads `locked`, and a run with the same code but without the recorded line reads `failed`.
+#[test]
+fn recorded_locked_case_through_runner() {
+    let fixture =
+        Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("../../tests/fixtures/helper/inno-locked");
+    let log = fs::read(fixture.join("log.txt")).expect("log.txt");
+    let exit: i32 = fs::read_to_string(fixture.join("exit_code.txt"))
+        .expect("exit_code.txt")
+        .trim()
+        .parse()
+        .expect("an integer exit code");
+    let other_log =
+        b"Log opened.\r\nDeleteFile failed; code 5.\r\nRolling back changes.\r\n".to_vec();
+    for (bytes, want) in [
+        (log, (OpOutcome::Locked, Some("E_HELPER_LOCKED"))),
+        (other_log, (OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_EXIT"))),
+    ] {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(Match::Any, writes_log(bytes, exit, false));
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[rig.inno("alpha.exe", b"alpha")],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(summary(&entries), [want]);
+        assert_eq!(entries[0].exit_code, Some(exit));
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn classify_sees_whole_bounded_log() {
+    // A matching line followed by 500 more: the classifier sees every line, the result keeps the last 40.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let mut lines = vec![
+            "Log opened.".to_owned(),
+            "MATCH: the file is in use".to_owned(),
+        ];
+        lines.extend((0..500).map(|n| format!("Rollback line {n}")));
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(
+            Match::Any,
+            writes_log(lines.join("\r\n").into_bytes(), 5, false),
+        );
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: recording_classifier,
+        };
+        SEEN.with(|s| s.borrow_mut().clear());
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[rig.inno("alpha.exe", b"alpha")],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_EXIT"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(entries[0].exit_code, Some(5));
+        let seen = SEEN.with(|s| s.borrow().clone());
+        assert_eq!(seen.len(), 1);
+        let at = seen[0]
+            .iter()
+            .position(|l| l == "MATCH: the file is in use")
+            .expect("the classifier saw the matching line");
+        assert_eq!(seen[0][at + 1..], lines[2..]);
+        assert_eq!(entries[0].log_tail, lines[lines.len() - 40..]);
+    }
+    // A 3 MiB log: only its last MiB reaches the classifier.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        // 64-byte records (63 + '\n'), no trailing newline: 3 MiB - 1 bytes.
+        let lines: Vec<String> = (0..49_152)
+            .map(|n| format!("{n:06} {}", "x".repeat(56)))
+            .collect();
+        let log = lines.join("\n");
+        assert_eq!(log.len(), 3 * 1024 * 1024 - 1);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(Match::Any, writes_log(log.into_bytes(), 0, false));
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: recording_classifier,
+        };
+        SEEN.with(|s| s.borrow_mut().clear());
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[rig.inno("alpha.exe", b"alpha")],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(summary(&entries), [(OpOutcome::Done, None)]);
+        let seen = SEEN.with(|s| s.borrow().clone());
+        assert_eq!(seen.len(), 1);
+        let handed = seen[0].join("\n").len();
+        assert!(
+            handed == 1024 * 1024 || handed == 1024 * 1024 - 1,
+            "the classifier was handed {handed} bytes"
+        );
+        assert!(!seen[0].contains(&lines[0]));
+        assert!(!seen[0].contains(&lines[32_767]));
+        assert!(seen[0].contains(&lines[32_768]));
+        assert_eq!(seen[0].last(), lines.last());
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn uninstall_polls_until_gone() {
+    let rig = Rig::new();
+    let world = World::new();
+    let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+    let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+    let alpha = rig.inno_install(&registry, APP_A, "Alpha");
+    vanish_at(&world, &alpha, Duration::from_secs(3));
+    let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+    let seams = Seams {
+        io: &io,
+        runner: &runner,
+        registry: &registry,
+        classify: classify_inno,
+    };
+    let entries = batch(
+        &rig,
+        HelperOs::Windows,
+        &[Op::RunInnoUninstaller {
+            app_id: APP_A.to_owned(),
+        }],
+        &seams,
+    );
+    assert_eq!(summary(&entries), [(OpOutcome::Done, None)]);
+    assert_eq!(entries[0].seconds, 3.0);
+    assert_eq!(count(&world, |c| matches!(c, Call::Sleep(_))), 6);
+    assert_eq!(
+        count(&world, |c| *c == Call::Sleep(UNINSTALL_POLL_INTERVAL)),
+        6
+    );
+    assert!(!alpha.install_dir.join("Alpha.vst3").exists());
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn uninstall_poll_times_out() {
+    // The uninstaller exits 0 but nothing ever disappears: 241 checks, 240 sleeps, 120 s on the fake clock.
+    {
+        let wall = Instant::now();
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let alpha = rig.inno_install(&registry, APP_A, "Alpha");
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[Op::RunInnoUninstaller {
+                app_id: APP_A.to_owned(),
+            }],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_UNINSTALL_TIMEOUT"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(entries[0].seconds, UNINSTALL_POLL_TIMEOUT.as_secs_f64());
+        assert_eq!(
+            count(&world, |c| *c == Call::Sleep(UNINSTALL_POLL_INTERVAL)),
+            240
+        );
+        assert_eq!(count(&world, |c| matches!(c, Call::Sleep(_))), 240);
+        assert_eq!(
+            count(&world, |c| *c == Call::KeyExists(alpha.subkey.clone())),
+            241
+        );
+        assert!(
+            wall.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(5),
+            "{:?}",
+            wall.elapsed()
+        );
+    }
+    // A non-zero exit: `failed E_HELPER_EXIT`, no poll.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let alpha = rig.inno_install(&registry, APP_A, "Alpha");
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(
+            Match::Any,
+            Script {
+                exit: 3,
+                ..Script::default()
+            },
+        );
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[Op::RunInnoUninstaller {
+                app_id: APP_A.to_owned(),
+            }],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_EXIT"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(entries[0].exit_code, Some(3));
+        assert_eq!(count(&world, |c| matches!(c, Call::Sleep(_))), 0);
+        assert_eq!(
+            count(&world, |c| *c == Call::KeyExists(alpha.subkey.clone())),
+            0
+        );
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn uninstaller_never_exits() {
+    let rig = Rig::new();
+    let world = World::new();
+    let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+    let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+    let alpha = rig.inno_install(&registry, APP_A, "Alpha");
+    let beta = rig.inno_install(&registry, APP_B, "Beta");
+    // Op 2 starts at 120 s (op 1's whole wait); its install vanishes a second later.
+    vanish_at(&world, &beta, Duration::from_secs(121));
+    let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+    runner.on(
+        Match::Nth(0),
+        Script {
+            never_exits: true,
+            ..Script::default()
+        },
+    );
+    let seams = Seams {
+        io: &io,
+        runner: &runner,
+        registry: &registry,
+        classify: classify_inno,
+    };
+    let ops = [APP_A, APP_B].map(|id| Op::RunInnoUninstaller {
+        app_id: id.to_owned(),
+    });
+    let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Windows, &ops, &seams);
+    assert_eq!(
+        summary(&entries),
+        [
+            (OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_UNINSTALL_TIMEOUT")),
+            (OpOutcome::Done, None),
+        ]
+    );
+    assert_eq!(entries[0].seconds, UNINSTALL_POLL_TIMEOUT.as_secs_f64());
+    assert_eq!(
+        count(
+            &world,
+            |c| matches!(c, Call::Wait { timeout, .. } if *timeout == UNINSTALL_POLL_TIMEOUT)
+        ),
+        2
+    );
+    assert_eq!(
+        count(&world, |c| *c == Call::KeyExists(alpha.subkey.clone())),
+        0,
+        "no poll follows an uninstaller that never exited"
+    );
+    assert_eq!(spawned(&world).len(), 2);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn runs_the_copy_never_the_original() {
+    // Rewritten right after the copy: the spawn names the run copy, whose hash is the job's.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (file, sha256) = rig.stage("alpha.exe", b"the verified setup");
+        world.hook(Hook::RewriteAfterCopy {
+            src: PathBuf::from(&file),
+            bytes: b"a tampered setup".to_vec(),
+        });
+        let digests = Mutex::new(Vec::new());
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(Match::Any, hashing(&digests));
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[Op::RunInnoInstaller {
+                file: file.clone(),
+                sha256,
+            }],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(summary(&entries), [(OpOutcome::Done, None)]);
+        let spawns = spawned(&world);
+        assert_eq!(spawns.len(), 1);
+        assert_eq!(
+            Path::new(&spawns[0][0]),
+            run_dir_of(&spawns[0]).join("installer-0.exe")
+        );
+        assert_eq!(*digests.lock().expect("digests"), [sha256]);
+        assert_eq!(
+            fs::read(&file).expect("the original"),
+            b"a tampered setup",
+            "the hook rewrote the original"
+        );
+    }
+    // Rewritten during the copy: the copy's digest differs, `E_HELPER_HASH`, nothing spawned, the copy removed.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (file, sha256) = rig.stage("alpha.exe", &vec![1u8; 200 * 1024]);
+        world.hook(Hook::RewriteDuringCopy {
+            src: PathBuf::from(&file),
+            bytes: vec![2u8; 200 * 1024],
+        });
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[Op::RunInnoInstaller { file, sha256 }],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_HASH"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(spawned(&world).len(), 0);
+        assert!(
+            count(
+                &world,
+                |c| matches!(c, Call::RemoveFile(p) if p.ends_with("installer-0.exe"))
+            ) >= 1
+        );
+    }
+    // Replaced by a link (to identical bytes) before the open: the no-follow open refuses it.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (file, sha256) = rig.stage("alpha.exe", b"the verified setup");
+        let (target, _) = rig.stage("twin.exe", b"the verified setup");
+        world.hook(Hook::LinkBeforeOpen {
+            src: PathBuf::from(&file),
+            target: PathBuf::from(target),
+        });
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[Op::RunInnoInstaller { file, sha256 }],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Refused, Some("E_HELPER_SOURCE"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(spawned(&world).len(), 0);
+    }
+    // Unix: a FIFO swapped in before the open neither blocks nor runs.
+    #[cfg(unix)]
+    {
+        let wall = Instant::now();
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (file, sha256) = rig.stage("alpha.pkg", b"pkg bytes");
+        rig.root_own(&world);
+        world.hook(Hook::FifoBeforeOpen {
+            src: PathBuf::from(&file),
+        });
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Macos,
+            &[Op::RunPkgInstaller { file, sha256 }],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Refused, Some("E_HELPER_SOURCE"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(spawned(&world).len(), 0);
+        assert!(
+            wall.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(1),
+            "{:?}",
+            wall.elapsed()
+        );
+    }
+}
+
+/// SC-006's receipt for `--install-location /`.
+const PKG_DIRS: [&str; 14] = [
+    "Library",
+    "Library/Audio",
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins",
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3",
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3",
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3/Contents",
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/Components",
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/Components/Alpha.component",
+    "Library/Application Support",
+    "Library/Application Support/Krate Audio",
+    "Library/Application Support/Krate Audio/Alpha",
+    "Applications",
+    "Applications/Alpha AUv3.app",
+    "Applications/Alpha AUv3.app/Contents",
+];
+const PKG_FILES: [&str; 4] = [
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3/Contents/Info.plist",
+    "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/Components/Alpha.component/Info.plist",
+    "Library/Application Support/Krate Audio/Alpha/Init.vstpreset",
+    "Applications/Alpha AUv3.app/Contents/Info.plist",
+];
+
+fn uninstall_a() -> [Op; 1] {
+    [Op::UninstallPkg {
+        pkg_id: PKG_A.to_owned(),
+    }]
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn pkg_paths_classified() {
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (files, dirs) = rig.receipt(&PKG_DIRS, &PKG_FILES);
+        let user = rig.on_vol("Library/Application Support/Krate Audio/Alpha/Mine.vstpreset");
+        fs::write(&user, b"the user's own preset").expect("plant a user preset");
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        script_receipt(&runner, PKG_A, info(PKG_A), files, dirs);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(summary(&entries), [(OpOutcome::Done, None)]);
+        for f in PKG_FILES {
+            assert!(!rig.on_vol(f).exists(), "{f} removed");
+        }
+        for d in [
+            "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3/Contents",
+            "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3",
+            "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/Components/Alpha.component",
+            "Applications/Alpha AUv3.app/Contents",
+            "Applications/Alpha AUv3.app",
+        ] {
+            assert!(!rig.on_vol(d).exists(), "{d} removed");
+        }
+        for d in [
+            "Library",
+            "Library/Audio",
+            "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins",
+            "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3",
+            "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/Components",
+            "Library/Application Support",
+            "Library/Application Support/Krate Audio",
+            "Applications",
+        ] {
+            assert!(rig.on_vol(d).is_dir(), "{d} is structural and kept");
+        }
+        assert!(
+            user.is_file(),
+            "the non-empty preset folder keeps the user's file"
+        );
+        let calls = world.calls();
+        let at = |rel: &str| position(&calls, |c| *c == Call::RemoveDir(rig.on_vol(rel)));
+        assert!(
+            at("Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3/Contents")
+                < at("Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3")
+        );
+        assert!(at("Applications/Alpha AUv3.app/Contents") < at("Applications/Alpha AUv3.app"));
+        let forget = pkgutil_argv(&["--forget", PKG_A]);
+        let all = launched(&world);
+        assert_eq!(all.iter().filter(|a| **a == forget).count(), 1);
+        assert_eq!(all.last(), Some(&forget), "--forget comes last");
+    }
+    // A directory outside every rule refuses the whole op; nothing is removed.
+    for stray in ["Applications/Other.app", "Applications/alpha AUv3.app"] {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let mut dirs = PKG_DIRS.to_vec();
+        dirs.push(stray);
+        let (files, dirs) = rig.receipt(&dirs, &PKG_FILES);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        script_receipt(&runner, PKG_A, info(PKG_A), files, dirs);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Refused, Some("E_HELPER_PKG_PATH"))],
+            "{stray}"
+        );
+        for f in PKG_FILES {
+            assert!(rig.on_vol(f).is_file(), "{stray}: {f} kept");
+        }
+        assert_eq!(
+            count(&world, |c| matches!(
+                c,
+                Call::RemoveFile(_) | Call::RemoveDir(_)
+            )),
+            0,
+            "{stray}"
+        );
+        assert!(!launched(&world).contains(&pkgutil_argv(&["--forget", PKG_A])));
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn pkg_failure_paths() {
+    let small_dirs = [
+        "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3",
+        "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3/Contents",
+    ];
+    let small_files = [
+        "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3/Contents/Info.plist",
+        "Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/VST3/Alpha.vst3/Contents/PkgInfo",
+    ];
+    let forget = pkgutil_argv(&["--forget", PKG_A]);
+    // No receipt: no `--files` read.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(
+            Match::Prefix(pkgutil_argv(&["--pkg-info", PKG_A])),
+            Script {
+                exit: 1,
+                ..Script::default()
+            },
+        );
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_PKG_RECEIPT"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(launched(&world), [pkgutil_argv(&["--pkg-info", PKG_A])]);
+    }
+    // The receipt names another package: nothing read further, nothing removed.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (files, dirs) = rig.receipt(&small_dirs, &small_files);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        script_receipt(&runner, PKG_A, info("com.krateaudio.beta"), files, dirs);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_PKG_RECEIPT"))]
+        );
+        assert_eq!(launched(&world), [pkgutil_argv(&["--pkg-info", PKG_A])]);
+        for f in small_files {
+            assert!(rig.on_vol(f).is_file(), "{f} kept");
+        }
+    }
+    // One file cannot be removed: `E_HELPER_REMOVE`, the rest removed, no `--forget`; the retry forgets once.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (files, dirs) = rig.receipt(&small_dirs, &small_files);
+        let stuck = rig.on_vol(small_files[1]);
+        world.fault(Fault::RemoveFile(stuck.clone()));
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        script_receipt(&runner, PKG_A, info(PKG_A), files, dirs);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_REMOVE"))]
+        );
+        assert!(!rig.on_vol(small_files[0]).exists());
+        assert!(stuck.is_file());
+        assert!(!launched(&world).contains(&forget));
+        world.clear_faults();
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(summary(&entries), [(OpOutcome::Done, None)]);
+        assert!(!stuck.exists());
+        assert_eq!(launched(&world).iter().filter(|a| **a == forget).count(), 1);
+    }
+    // `--forget` exits non-zero.
+    {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (files, dirs) = rig.receipt(&small_dirs, &small_files);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        script_receipt(&runner, PKG_A, info(PKG_A), files, dirs);
+        runner.on(
+            Match::Prefix(forget.clone()),
+            Script {
+                exit: 1,
+                ..Script::default()
+            },
+        );
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_FORGET"))]
+        );
+        for f in small_files {
+            assert!(!rig.on_vol(f).exists(), "{f} removed");
+        }
+    }
+    // A `pkgutil` that cannot be started is `E_HELPER_SPAWN` (plan SI-12), at each of its four runs; the rule goes
+    // before the receipt's own, so it wins. Nothing is removed before `--forget`; the forget leg has removed the files.
+    for (args, removed) in [
+        (vec!["--pkg-info", PKG_A], false),
+        (vec!["--files", PKG_A, "--only-files"], false),
+        (vec!["--files", PKG_A, "--only-dirs"], false),
+        (vec!["--forget", PKG_A], true),
+    ] {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let (files, dirs) = rig.receipt(&small_dirs, &small_files);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(
+            Match::Prefix(pkgutil_argv(&args)),
+            Script {
+                spawn_error: Some(io::ErrorKind::NotFound),
+                ..Script::default()
+            },
+        );
+        script_receipt(&runner, PKG_A, info(PKG_A), files, dirs);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, HelperOs::Macos, &uninstall_a(), &seams);
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [(OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_SPAWN"))],
+            "{args:?}"
+        );
+        assert_eq!(launched(&world).last(), Some(&pkgutil_argv(&args)));
+        for f in small_files {
+            assert_eq!(!rig.on_vol(f).exists(), removed, "{args:?}: {f}");
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn run_dir_removed_after_job() {
+    let both: &[&str] = &["installer-0.exe", "inno-0.log"];
+    let rows: [(&str, Script<'static>, OpOutcome, &[&str]); 3] = [
+        (
+            "done",
+            writes_log(b"Installed.".to_vec(), 0, false),
+            OpOutcome::Done,
+            both,
+        ),
+        (
+            "failed",
+            writes_log(b"Failed.".to_vec(), 1, false),
+            OpOutcome::Failed,
+            both,
+        ),
+        // The still-running installer may not have written its log yet; its copy is removed either way.
+        (
+            "timed out",
+            Script {
+                never_exits: true,
+                ..Script::default()
+            },
+            OpOutcome::Failed,
+            &["installer-0.exe"],
+        ),
+    ];
+    for (name, script, outcome, files) in rows {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        runner.on(Match::Any, script);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let entries = batch(
+            &rig,
+            HelperOs::Windows,
+            &[rig.inno("alpha.exe", b"alpha")],
+            &seams,
+        );
+        assert_eq!(entries[0].outcome, outcome, "{name}");
+        let calls = world.calls();
+        let Some(Call::Spawn(argv)) = calls.iter().find(|c| matches!(c, Call::Spawn(_))) else {
+            panic!("{name}: no spawn");
+        };
+        let run = run_dir_of(argv);
+        let dir = position(&calls, |c| *c == Call::RemoveDir(run.clone()));
+        for file in files {
+            let at = position(&calls, |c| *c == Call::RemoveFile(run.join(file)));
+            assert!(at < dir, "{name}: {file} removed before the directory");
+        }
+        assert!(!run.exists(), "{name}: the run directory is gone");
+    }
+}
+
+/// Builds one SC-027 defect into the macOS run-directory chain; the rest is root-owned.
+fn chain_defect(rig: &Rig, world: &World, defect: &str) {
+    let library = &rig.mac.run_ancestors[0];
+    let support = &rig.mac.run_ancestors[1];
+    match defect {
+        "uid 501" => {
+            world.owner(library, 501, 0o755);
+            world.root_owned(support);
+            world.root_owned(&rig.mac.run_base);
+        }
+        "group-writable" | "world-writable" => {
+            world.root_owned(library);
+            let mode = if defect == "group-writable" {
+                0o775
+            } else {
+                0o757
+            };
+            world.owner(support, 0, mode);
+            world.root_owned(&rig.mac.run_base);
+        }
+        "symlink" => {
+            world.root_owned(library);
+            world.root_owned(support);
+            let real = support.join("elsewhere");
+            mkdirs(&real);
+            world.root_owned(&real);
+            fs::remove_dir(&rig.mac.run_base).expect("remove the helper folder");
+            #[cfg(unix)]
+            std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&real, &rig.mac.run_base)
+                .expect("symbolic-link privilege not held: enable Developer Mode");
+            #[cfg(windows)]
+            std::os::windows::fs::symlink_dir(&real, &rig.mac.run_base)
+                .expect("symbolic-link privilege not held: enable Developer Mode");
+        }
+        _ => {
+            rig.root_own(world);
+            world.fault(Fault::CreateRunDir(io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists));
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn run_dir_refusals_spawn_nothing() {
+    let rows = [
+        (HelperOs::Macos, "uid 501"),
+        (HelperOs::Macos, "group-writable"),
+        (HelperOs::Macos, "world-writable"),
+        (HelperOs::Macos, "symlink"),
+        (HelperOs::Macos, "existing name"),
+        (HelperOs::Windows, "existing name"),
+    ];
+    for (os, defect) in rows {
+        let rig = Rig::new();
+        let world = World::new();
+        if os == HelperOs::Macos {
+            chain_defect(&rig, &world, defect);
+        } else {
+            world.fault(Fault::CreateRunDir(io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists));
+        }
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, &rig.base);
+        let registry = FakeRegistry::new(&world);
+        let runner = ScriptedRunner::new(&world);
+        let seams = Seams {
+            io: &io,
+            runner: &runner,
+            registry: &registry,
+            classify: classify_inno,
+        };
+        let ops = match os {
+            HelperOs::Windows => [
+                rig.inno("alpha.exe", b"alpha"),
+                rig.inno("beta.exe", b"beta"),
+            ],
+            _ => [rig.pkg("alpha.pkg", b"alpha"), rig.pkg("beta.pkg", b"beta")],
+        };
+        let entries = batch(&rig, os, &ops, &seams);
+        assert_eq!(
+            summary(&entries),
+            [
+                (OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_RUN_DIR")),
+                (OpOutcome::Failed, Some("E_HELPER_RUN_DIR")),
+            ],
+            "{os:?} {defect}"
+        );
+        assert_eq!(spawned(&world).len(), 0, "{os:?} {defect}: zero spawns");
+        assert_eq!(
+            count(&world, |c| matches!(c, Call::CreateNew(_))),
+            0,
+            "{os:?} {defect}: zero copies"
+        );
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+#[should_panic(expected = "outside the test sandbox")]
+fn temp_io_refuses_outside_base() {
+    let a = TempDir::new().expect("temp dir A");
+    let b = TempDir::new().expect("temp dir B");
+    let world = World::new();
+    let io = TempIo::new(&world, a.path());
+    let _ = io.create_run_dir(&b.path().join("run-0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef"));
+}
+
+fn panic_text(payload: &(dyn Any + Send)) -> String {
+    payload
+        .downcast_ref::<&str>()
+        .map(|s| (*s).to_owned())
+        .or_else(|| payload.downcast_ref::<String>().cloned())
+        .unwrap_or_default()
+}
+
+/// `temp_io_refuses_outside_base`'s companion rows: every other mutating method and every source read outside the
+/// base panics too, and the other temp dir is untouched.
+#[test]
+fn temp_io_refuses_every_other_method_outside_base() {
+    let a = TempDir::new().expect("temp dir A");
+    let b = TempDir::new().expect("temp dir B");
+    let victim = b.path().join("victim.bin");
+    fs::write(&victim, b"keep").expect("write the victim");
+    let sub = b.path().join("sub");
+    mkdirs(&sub);
+    let new_file = b.path().join("new.bin");
+    let helper = b.path().join("helper");
+    type Row<'a> = (&'static str, Box<dyn Fn(&dyn HelperIo) + 'a>);
+    let rows: [Row<'_>; 7] = [
+        (
+            "create_new",
+            Box::new(|io| {
+                let _ = io.create_new(&new_file);
+            }),
+        ),
+        (
+            "create_helper_folder",
+            Box::new(|io| {
+                let _ = io.create_helper_folder(&helper);
+            }),
+        ),
+        (
+            "remove_file",
+            Box::new(|io| {
+                let _ = io.remove_file(&victim);
+            }),
+        ),
+        (
+            "remove_dir",
+            Box::new(|io| {
+                let _ = io.remove_dir(&sub);
+            }),
+        ),
+        (
+            "open_source",
+            Box::new(|io| {
+                let _ = io.open_source(&victim);
+            }),
+        ),
+        (
+            "read_bounded",
+            Box::new(|io| {
+                let _ = io.read_bounded(&victim, 16);
+            }),
+        ),
+        (
+            "read_tail",
+            Box::new(|io| {
+                let _ = io.read_tail(&victim, 16);
+            }),
+        ),
+    ];
+    for (name, call) in rows {
+        let world = World::new();
+        let io = TempIo::new(&world, a.path());
+        let payload = catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(|| call(&io)))
+            .expect_err(&format!("{name} outside the base must panic"));
+        assert!(
+            panic_text(&*payload).contains("outside the test sandbox"),
+            "{name}: {:?}",
+            panic_text(&*payload)
+        );
+    }
+    assert_eq!(fs::read(&victim).expect("the victim"), b"keep");
+    assert!(sub.is_dir());
+    let mut left: Vec<OsString> = fs::read_dir(b.path())
+        .expect("list B")
+        .map(|e| e.expect("entry").file_name())
+        .collect();
+    left.sort();
+    assert_eq!(left, strs(&["sub", "victim.bin"]));
+}

+//! The real seams (plan §4.1.12; PD-6, PD-7): `cfg(unix)` / `cfg(windows)`, never `cfg(target_os = "macos")`.
+//! Constructed only in `main.rs` and `os_tests.rs` (FR-053, SI-25). The helper's only `Instant` home (FR-041).
+
+use crate::seams::{ChildId, HelperIo, ProcessRunner, Registry, WriteSync};
+use crate::validate::{Kind, LstatFacts};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+use std::collections::hash_map::RandomState;
+use std::ffi::OsString;
+use std::fs::{File, OpenOptions};
+use std::hash::BuildHasher;
+use std::io::{self, Read, Seek, SeekFrom, Write};
+use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
+use std::process::{Child, Command, ExitStatus, Stdio};

+/// `open(2)` flags for PD-6, from `libc-0.2.191` (the workspace has no `libc`): Apple `src/unix/bsd/mod.rs:275-276`.
+#[cfg(all(unix, target_vendor = "apple"))]
+const O_NONBLOCK: i32 = 0x4;
+#[cfg(all(unix, target_vendor = "apple"))]
+const O_NOFOLLOW: i32 = 0x100;
+/// Linux x86_64: `src/unix/linux_like/linux/gnu/b64/x86_64/mod.rs:294`, `:448`.
+#[cfg(all(target_os = "linux", target_arch = "x86_64"))]
+const O_NONBLOCK: i32 = 2048;
+#[cfg(all(target_os = "linux", target_arch = "x86_64"))]
+const O_NOFOLLOW: i32 = 0x20000;
+/// Linux aarch64: `src/unix/linux_like/linux/gnu/b64/aarch64/mod.rs:206`, `:359`.
+#[cfg(all(target_os = "linux", target_arch = "aarch64"))]
+const O_NONBLOCK: i32 = 2048;
+#[cfg(all(target_os = "linux", target_arch = "aarch64"))]
+const O_NOFOLLOW: i32 = 0x8000;
+#[cfg(all(
+    unix,
+    not(target_vendor = "apple"),
+    not(all(
+        target_os = "linux",
+        any(target_arch = "x86_64", target_arch = "aarch64")
+    ))
+))]
+compile_error!(
+    "os.rs: O_NONBLOCK / O_NOFOLLOW are defined for Apple and Linux x86_64 / aarch64 only (PD-6)"
+);
+
+/// How often `OsRunner` polls a child (plan §4.1.12).
+const POLL: Duration = Duration::from_millis(100);
+
+/// The process counter `random_hex` hashes (PD-19).
+static NAME_COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
+/// The number of the next spawned child.
+static NEXT_CHILD: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
+
+/// The Windows known folders the production roots use (tasks TA-6); mapped to `FOLDERID_*` only here.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub enum KnownFolder {
+    ProgramFilesCommon,
+    ProgramData,
+    UserProfiles,
+    Windows,
+}
+
+/// The known folder's path, `None` when the lookup fails (FR-051).
+#[cfg(windows)]
+pub fn known_folder(id: KnownFolder) -> Option<PathBuf> {
+    windows::known_folder(id)
+}

+/// The refusal of a path that is not a regular file reached without a final link.
+fn not_regular(path: &Path) -> io::Error {
+    io::Error::new(
+        io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
+        format!(
+            "not a regular file opened without a link: {}",
+            path.display()
+        ),
+    )
+}
+
+/// PD-6: open `path` for reading without following a final link and without blocking; a regular file only.
+#[cfg(unix)]
+pub fn open_regular_no_follow(path: &Path) -> io::Result<File> {
+    use std::os::unix::fs::{MetadataExt, OpenOptionsExt};
+    let file = OpenOptions::new()
+        .read(true)
+        .custom_flags(O_NONBLOCK | O_NOFOLLOW)
+        .open(path)?;
+    let opened = file.metadata()?;
+    let named = std::fs::symlink_metadata(path)?;
+    if !opened.file_type().is_file() || opened.dev() != named.dev() || opened.ino() != named.ino() {
+        return Err(not_regular(path));
+    }
+    Ok(file)
+}
+
+/// PD-6: open `path` for reading without following a final link; a regular file only.
+#[cfg(windows)]
+pub fn open_regular_no_follow(path: &Path) -> io::Result<File> {
+    use std::os::windows::fs::OpenOptionsExt;
+    let file = OpenOptions::new()
+        .read(true)
+        .custom_flags(windows::FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT)
+        .open(path)?;
+    let kind = file.metadata()?.file_type();
+    if kind.is_symlink() || !kind.is_file() {
+        return Err(not_regular(path));
+    }
+    Ok(file)
+}
+
+/// A created file whose bytes can be made durable.
+struct SyncFile(File);
+
+impl Write for SyncFile {
+    fn write(&mut self, buf: &[u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
+        self.0.write(buf)
+    }
+
+    fn flush(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
+        self.0.flush()
+    }
+}
+
+impl WriteSync for SyncFile {
+    fn sync_all(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
+        self.0.sync_all()
+    }
+}
+
+/// The real filesystem, clock and randomness.
+pub struct OsHelperIo {
+    origin: Instant,
+}
+
+impl OsHelperIo {
+    pub fn new() -> OsHelperIo {
+        OsHelperIo {
+            origin: Instant::now(),
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+impl Default for OsHelperIo {
+    fn default() -> OsHelperIo {
+        OsHelperIo::new()
+    }
+}
+
+/// `symlink_metadata`'s kind, a link first.
+fn kind_of(meta: &std::fs::Metadata) -> Kind {
+    let t = meta.file_type();
+    if t.is_symlink() {
+        Kind::Symlink
+    } else if t.is_dir() {
+        Kind::Dir
+    } else if t.is_file() {
+        Kind::File
+    } else {
+        Kind::Other
+    }
+}
+
+/// The owner and the permission bits on Unix.
+#[cfg(unix)]
+fn owner_of(meta: &std::fs::Metadata) -> (Option<u32>, Option<u32>) {
+    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
+    (Some(meta.uid()), Some(meta.mode() & 0o7777))
+}
+
+/// Windows reports no uid / mode (`LstatFacts`).
+#[cfg(windows)]
+fn owner_of(_meta: &std::fs::Metadata) -> (Option<u32>, Option<u32>) {
+    (None, None)
+}
+
+/// A directory with `mode` (before the umask); fails on an existing name.
+#[cfg(unix)]
+fn create_dir_mode(path: &Path, mode: u32) -> io::Result<()> {
+    use std::os::unix::fs::DirBuilderExt;
+    std::fs::DirBuilder::new().mode(mode).create(path)
+}
+
+impl HelperIo for OsHelperIo {
+    fn read_bounded(&self, path: &Path, max: u64) -> io::Result<Vec<u8>> {
+        let mut bytes = Vec::new();
+        open_regular_no_follow(path)?
+            .take(max.saturating_add(1))
+            .read_to_end(&mut bytes)?;
+        Ok(bytes)
+    }
+
+    fn read_tail(&self, path: &Path, max: u64) -> io::Result<Vec<u8>> {
+        let mut file = open_regular_no_follow(path)?;
+        let len = file.metadata()?.len();
+        file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(len.saturating_sub(max)))?;
+        let mut bytes = Vec::new();
+        file.take(max).read_to_end(&mut bytes)?;
+        Ok(bytes)
+    }
+
+    fn canonicalize(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
+        std::fs::canonicalize(path)
+    }
+
+    fn lstat(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<Option<LstatFacts>> {
+        let meta = match std::fs::symlink_metadata(path) {
+            Ok(meta) => meta,
+            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(None),
+            Err(e) => return Err(e),
+        };
+        let (uid, mode) = owner_of(&meta);
+        Ok(Some(LstatFacts {
+            kind: kind_of(&meta),
+            uid,
+            mode,
+        }))
+    }
+
+    fn open_source(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<Box<dyn Read + '_>> {
+        Ok(Box::new(open_regular_no_follow(path)?))
+    }
+
+    fn create_new(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<Box<dyn WriteSync + '_>> {
+        let file = OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true).open(path)?;
+        Ok(Box::new(SyncFile(file)))
+    }
+
+    fn create_helper_folder(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
+        #[cfg(unix)]
+        let made = create_dir_mode(path, 0o755);
+        #[cfg(windows)]
+        let made = std::fs::create_dir(path);
+        match made {
+            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists => Ok(()),
+            other => other,
+        }
+    }
+
+    #[cfg(unix)]
+    fn create_run_dir(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
+        create_dir_mode(path, 0o700)
+    }
+
+    #[cfg(windows)]
+    fn create_run_dir(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
+        windows::create_run_dir(path)
+    }
+
+    fn list_dir(&self, dir: &Path) -> io::Result<Vec<OsString>> {
+        std::fs::read_dir(dir)?
+            .map(|entry| entry.map(|e| e.file_name()))
+            .collect()
+    }
+
+    fn remove_file(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
+        std::fs::remove_file(path)
+    }
+
+    fn remove_dir(&self, path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
+        std::fs::remove_dir(path)
+    }
+
+    fn random_hex(&self) -> String {
+        let n = NAME_COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
+        let pid = std::process::id();
+        let high = RandomState::new().hash_one((n, pid));
+        let low = RandomState::new().hash_one((pid, n));
+        format!("{high:016x}{low:016x}")
+    }
+
+    fn sleep(&self, d: Duration) {
+        std::thread::sleep(d);
+    }
+
+    fn now(&self) -> Duration {
+        self.origin.elapsed()
+    }
+}
+
+/// A child's exit code: its code, else `128 + signal` (Unix).
+#[cfg(unix)]
+fn exit_code(status: ExitStatus) -> i32 {
+    use std::os::unix::process::ExitStatusExt;
+    status
+        .code()
+        .or_else(|| status.signal().map(|s| 128 + s))
+        .unwrap_or(-1)
+}
+
+/// A child's exit code (always present on Windows).
+#[cfg(windows)]
+fn exit_code(status: ExitStatus) -> i32 {
+    status.code().unwrap_or(-1)
+}
+
+/// `argv[0]` with `argv[1..]`, never a shell; stdin and stderr null.
+fn command(argv: &[OsString], stdout: Stdio) -> io::Result<Command> {
+    let Some((program, args)) = argv.split_first() else {
+        return Err(io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput, "empty argv"));
+    };
+    let mut command = Command::new(program);
+    command
+        .args(args)
+        .stdin(Stdio::null())
+        .stdout(stdout)
+        .stderr(Stdio::null());
+    Ok(command)
+}
+
+/// Real child processes: `Command::new(argv[0])`, all stdio null, never a shell, never a kill (FR-042).
+pub struct OsRunner {
+    children: Mutex<BTreeMap<u64, Child>>,
+}
+
+impl OsRunner {
+    pub fn new() -> OsRunner {
+        OsRunner {
+            children: Mutex::new(BTreeMap::new()),
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn children(&self) -> MutexGuard<'_, BTreeMap<u64, Child>> {
+        self.children.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)
+    }
+}
+
+impl Default for OsRunner {
+    fn default() -> OsRunner {
+        OsRunner::new()
+    }
+}
+
+impl ProcessRunner for OsRunner {
+    fn spawn(&self, argv: &[OsString]) -> io::Result<ChildId> {
+        let child = command(argv, Stdio::null())?.spawn()?;
+        let id = NEXT_CHILD.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
+        self.children().insert(id, child);
+        Ok(ChildId(id))
+    }
+
+    fn wait(&self, child: ChildId, timeout: Duration) -> io::Result<Option<i32>> {
+        let start = Instant::now();
+        loop {
+            {
+                let mut children = self.children();
+                let Some(running) = children.get_mut(&child.0) else {
+                    return Err(io::Error::new(io::ErrorKind::NotFound, "no such child"));
+                };
+                if let Some(status) = running.try_wait()? {
+                    children.remove(&child.0);
+                    return Ok(Some(exit_code(status)));
+                }
+            }
+            let elapsed = start.elapsed();
+            if elapsed >= timeout {
+                return Ok(None);
+            }
+            std::thread::sleep(POLL.min(timeout - elapsed));
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn output(&self, argv: &[OsString], timeout: Duration) -> io::Result<Option<(i32, Vec<u8>)>> {
+        let start = Instant::now();
+        let mut child = command(argv, Stdio::piped())?.spawn()?;
+        let Some(mut stdout) = child.stdout.take() else {
+            return Err(io::Error::other("the child's stdout was not piped"));
+        };
+        // The pipe is drained beside the wait, so a child that fills it never blocks; a reader left behind by a
+        // timeout ends when the child closes its end.
+        let (send, receive) = mpsc::channel();
+        std::thread::spawn(move || {
+            let mut bytes = Vec::new();
+            let read = stdout.read_to_end(&mut bytes).map(|_| bytes);
+            // The receiver is gone only after a timeout, when nobody wants the bytes.
+            let _ = send.send(read);
+        });
+        let status = loop {
+            if let Some(status) = child.try_wait()? {
+                break status;
+            }
+            let elapsed = start.elapsed();
+            if elapsed >= timeout {
+                return Ok(None);
+            }
+            std::thread::sleep(POLL.min(timeout - elapsed));
+        };
+        let left = timeout.saturating_sub(start.elapsed());
+        match receive.recv_timeout(left) {
+            Ok(read) => Ok(Some((exit_code(status), read?))),
+            Err(mpsc::RecvTimeoutError::Timeout) => Ok(None),
+            Err(mpsc::RecvTimeoutError::Disconnected) => {
+                Err(io::Error::other("the stdout reader ended without a result"))
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// HKLM, 64-bit view, on Windows; `None` / `false` elsewhere.
+pub struct OsRegistry;
+
+/// The two values FR-031 reads from an uninstall key.
+#[cfg(windows)]
+const UNINSTALL_VALUES: [&str; 2] = ["UninstallString", "InstallLocation"];
+
+impl Registry for OsRegistry {
+    #[cfg(windows)]
+    fn uninstall_values(&self, subkey: &str) -> Option<(Option<String>, Option<String>)> {
+        let key = windows::OpenKey::open(subkey).ok()??;
+        let [uninstall_string, install_location] = UNINSTALL_VALUES;
+        Some((key.read_sz(uninstall_string), key.read_sz(install_location)))
+    }
+
+    #[cfg(not(windows))]
+    fn uninstall_values(&self, _subkey: &str) -> Option<(Option<String>, Option<String>)> {
+        None
+    }
+
+    /// A key that cannot be opened for a reason other than its absence counts as present, so the uninstall poll
+    /// never reads a failure as a removal.
+    #[cfg(windows)]
+    fn key_exists(&self, subkey: &str) -> bool {
+        !matches!(windows::OpenKey::open(subkey), Ok(None))
+    }
+
+    #[cfg(not(windows))]
+    fn key_exists(&self, _subkey: &str) -> bool {
+        false
+    }
+}
+
+/// The Windows FFI of the real seams: the run directory's DACL, known folders, HKLM reads.
+#[cfg(windows)]
+mod windows {
+    use super::KnownFolder;
+    use crate::validate::RUN_DIR_SDDL;
+    use std::ffi::OsString;
+    use std::io;
+    use std::os::windows::ffi::{OsStrExt, OsStringExt};
+    use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
+    use std::ptr::{null, null_mut};
+    use windows_sys::Win32::Foundation::{ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND, ERROR_SUCCESS, LocalFree};
+    use windows_sys::Win32::Security::Authorization::{
+        ConvertStringSecurityDescriptorToSecurityDescriptorW, SDDL_REVISION_1,
+    };
+    use windows_sys::Win32::Security::{PSECURITY_DESCRIPTOR, SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES};
+    use windows_sys::Win32::Storage::FileSystem::CreateDirectoryW;
+    pub use windows_sys::Win32::Storage::FileSystem::FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT;
+    use windows_sys::Win32::System::Com::CoTaskMemFree;
+    use windows_sys::Win32::System::Registry::{
+        HKEY, HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, KEY_READ, KEY_WOW64_64KEY, RRF_RT_REG_SZ, RegCloseKey,
+        RegGetValueW, RegOpenKeyExW,
+    };
+    use windows_sys::Win32::UI::Shell::{
+        FOLDERID_ProgramData, FOLDERID_ProgramFilesCommon, FOLDERID_UserProfiles, FOLDERID_Windows,
+        KF_FLAG_DEFAULT, SHGetKnownFolderPath,
+    };
+
+    /// UTF-16 plus a NUL.
+    fn wide(s: &str) -> Vec<u16> {
+        s.encode_utf16().chain(Some(0)).collect()
+    }
+
+    /// A path as UTF-16 plus a NUL; an interior NUL would cut the path short, so it is refused.
+    fn wide_path(path: &Path) -> io::Result<Vec<u16>> {
+        let units: Vec<u16> = path.as_os_str().encode_wide().collect();
+        if units.contains(&0) {
+            return Err(io::Error::new(
+                io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
+                "a NUL in the path",
+            ));
+        }
+        Ok(units.into_iter().chain(Some(0)).collect())
+    }
+
+    /// D7: the directory is created with `RUN_DIR_SDDL` as its DACL (protected: SYSTEM and Administrators only);
+    /// it fails on an existing name.
+    pub fn create_run_dir(path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
+        let path = wide_path(path)?;
+        let sddl = wide(RUN_DIR_SDDL);
+        let mut descriptor: PSECURITY_DESCRIPTOR = null_mut();
+        // SAFETY: `sddl` is NUL-terminated and outlives the call; `descriptor` is a valid out pointer that receives
+        // a LocalAlloc'd descriptor, freed by the LocalFree below on every path after a success; the size out
+        // pointer may be NULL (the ConvertStringSecurityDescriptorToSecurityDescriptorW page).
+        let converted = unsafe {
+            ConvertStringSecurityDescriptorToSecurityDescriptorW(
+                sddl.as_ptr(),
+                SDDL_REVISION_1,
+                &mut descriptor,
+                null_mut(),
+            )
+        };
+        if converted == 0 {
+            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
+        }
+        let attributes = SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES {
+            nLength: size_of::<SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES>() as u32,
+            lpSecurityDescriptor: descriptor,
+            bInheritHandle: 0,
+        };
+        // SAFETY: `path` is NUL-terminated and outlives the call; `attributes` points at the descriptor converted
+        // above, which stays allocated until the LocalFree after this call.
+        let created = unsafe { CreateDirectoryW(path.as_ptr(), &attributes) };
+        let outcome = if created == 0 {
+            Err(io::Error::last_os_error())
+        } else {
+            Ok(())
+        };
+        // SAFETY: `descriptor` is the LocalAlloc'd block the conversion returned, freed exactly once here and not
+        // used afterwards; LocalFree's return (NULL on success) carries nothing to act on.
+        unsafe { LocalFree(descriptor) };
+        outcome
+    }
+
+    /// TA-6: `SHGetKnownFolderPath` for `id`; the returned buffer is freed with `CoTaskMemFree` on every path.
+    pub fn known_folder(id: KnownFolder) -> Option<PathBuf> {
+        let guid = match id {
+            KnownFolder::ProgramFilesCommon => FOLDERID_ProgramFilesCommon,
+            KnownFolder::ProgramData => FOLDERID_ProgramData,
+            KnownFolder::UserProfiles => FOLDERID_UserProfiles,
+            KnownFolder::Windows => FOLDERID_Windows,
+        };
+        let mut buffer: *mut u16 = null_mut();
+        // SAFETY: `guid` outlives the call; a NULL token names the current user; `buffer` is a valid out pointer
+        // that receives a CoTaskMemAlloc'd, NUL-terminated string, which the caller frees with CoTaskMemFree
+        // whether the call succeeds or not (the SHGetKnownFolderPath page).
+        let hr =
+            unsafe { SHGetKnownFolderPath(&guid, KF_FLAG_DEFAULT as u32, null_mut(), &mut buffer) };
+        let path = if hr >= 0 && !buffer.is_null() {
+            let mut len = 0usize;
+            // SAFETY: on success `buffer` is a NUL-terminated UTF-16 string, so every unit up to and including
+            // the NUL is readable.
+            while unsafe { *buffer.add(len) } != 0 {
+                len += 1;
+            }
+            // SAFETY: the `len` units before the NUL were just read one by one; the buffer is not freed yet.
+            let units = unsafe { std::slice::from_raw_parts(buffer, len) };
+            Some(PathBuf::from(OsString::from_wide(units)))
+        } else {
+            None
+        };
+        // SAFETY: `buffer` is NULL or the block SHGetKnownFolderPath allocated; CoTaskMemFree accepts NULL, the
+        // block is freed exactly once here, and `units` (the only view of it) is dropped above.
+        unsafe { CoTaskMemFree(buffer.cast()) };
+        path
+    }
+
+    /// An HKLM key opened in the 64-bit view, closed exactly once when dropped.
+    pub struct OpenKey(HKEY);
+
+    impl Drop for OpenKey {
+        fn drop(&mut self) {
+            // SAFETY: `self.0` is the handle a successful RegOpenKeyExW returned; only `OpenKey::open` builds an
+            // `OpenKey`, once per open, and nothing uses the handle after this drop. The code carries nothing to
+            // act on.
+            unsafe { RegCloseKey(self.0) };
+        }
+    }
+
+    impl OpenKey {
+        /// `Ok(None)` when the key does not exist; `Err` for any other failure.
+        pub fn open(subkey: &str) -> Result<Option<OpenKey>, u32> {
+            let subkey = wide(subkey);
+            let mut hkey: HKEY = null_mut();
+            // SAFETY: `subkey` is NUL-terminated and outlives the call; `hkey` is a valid out pointer that
+            // receives the opened handle, which `OpenKey` closes on drop (the RegOpenKeyExW page).
+            let rc = unsafe {
+                RegOpenKeyExW(
+                    HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE,
+                    subkey.as_ptr(),
+                    0,
+                    KEY_READ | KEY_WOW64_64KEY,
+                    &mut hkey,
+                )
+            };
+            match rc {
+                ERROR_SUCCESS => Ok(Some(OpenKey(hkey))),
+                ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND => Ok(None),
+                other => Err(other),
+            }
+        }
+
+        /// A `REG_SZ` value; `None` when absent or unreadable.
+        pub fn read_sz(&self, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
+            let name = wide(name);
+            let mut size: u32 = 0;
+            // SAFETY: `self.0` is open; `name` is NUL-terminated and outlives the call; a NULL subkey reads the key
+            // itself; with a NULL data pointer the call only stores the value's size in bytes in `size` (the
+            // RegGetValueW page).
+            let rc = unsafe {
+                RegGetValueW(
+                    self.0,
+                    null(),
+                    name.as_ptr(),
+                    RRF_RT_REG_SZ,
+                    null_mut(),
+                    null_mut(),
+                    &mut size,
+                )
+            };
+            if rc != ERROR_SUCCESS {
+                return None;
+            }
+            let mut data = vec![0u16; (size as usize).div_ceil(2)];
+            // SAFETY: `data` holds at least `size` bytes and is writable for the call; `size` says so, and the call
+            // writes at most that many bytes (it fails with ERROR_MORE_DATA instead of writing past it).
+            let rc = unsafe {
+                RegGetValueW(
+                    self.0,
+                    null(),
+                    name.as_ptr(),
+                    RRF_RT_REG_SZ,
+                    null_mut(),
+                    data.as_mut_ptr().cast(),
+                    &mut size,
+                )
+            };
+            if rc != ERROR_SUCCESS {
+                return None;
+            }
+            let len = data
+                .iter()
+                .position(|&unit| unit == 0)
+                .unwrap_or(data.len());
+            Some(String::from_utf16_lossy(&data[..len]))
+        }
+    }
+}

+//! `os::tests` (owner T008; plan §4.1.12, §7.1; SI-25): the real seams, unelevated, over a `TempDir`. The one test
+//! source that may build `OsHelperIo` and `OsRunner`; no row names a production path.

+fn temp() -> tempfile::TempDir {
+    tempfile::tempdir().expect("a temp dir")

+#[test]
+fn read_bounded_stops_at_bound() {
+    const MAX: u64 = 4096;
+    let td = temp();
+    let path = td.path().join("big.bin");
+    let bytes: Vec<u8> = (0..2 * MAX).map(|i| (i % 251) as u8).collect();
+    std::fs::write(&path, &bytes).expect("write the 2 x MAX file");
+    let io = OsHelperIo::new();
+    let max = usize::try_from(MAX).expect("MAX fits usize");
+
+    let head = io.read_bounded(&path, MAX).expect("read_bounded");
+    assert_eq!(head.len(), max + 1, "read_bounded reads MAX + 1 bytes");
+    assert_eq!(head, bytes[..=max], "read_bounded reads from the start");
+
+    let tail = io.read_tail(&path, MAX).expect("read_tail");
+    assert_eq!(tail.len(), max, "read_tail reads MAX bytes");
+    assert_eq!(tail, bytes[max..], "read_tail reads the last MAX bytes");
+
+    let short = td.path().join("short.bin");
+    std::fs::write(&short, b"abc").expect("write the short file");
+    assert_eq!(io.read_bounded(&short, MAX).expect("short head"), b"abc");
+    assert_eq!(io.read_tail(&short, MAX).expect("short tail"), b"abc");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn random_hex_is_32_lower_hex_and_varies() {

+        assert_eq!(name.len(), 32, "{name:?} is 32 digits");
+        assert!(
+            name.bytes()
+                .all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit() || (b'a'..=b'f').contains(&b)),
+            "{name:?} is lower-case hex"
+        );
+    }
+    assert_ne!(first, second, "two calls differ");
+}

+    assert_eq!(facts.mode.is_some(), cfg!(unix), "mode only on Unix");

+    assert_eq!(dir.kind, Kind::Dir);
+    assert_eq!(
+        io.lstat(&td.path().join("absent")).expect("lstat absent"),
+        None
+    );

+fn open_source_refuses_link_windows() {
+    use super::open_regular_no_follow;
+    use std::io::Read;
+    let td = temp();
+    let target = td.path().join("target.bin");
+    std::fs::write(&target, b"payload").expect("write the target");
+    let link = td.path().join("link.bin");
+    std::os::windows::fs::symlink_file(&target, &link).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
+        panic!("symbolic-link privilege not held: enable Developer Mode ({e})")
+    });
+    let io = OsHelperIo::new();
+
+    assert!(
+        open_regular_no_follow(&link).is_err(),
+        "the link is refused"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        io.open_source(&link).is_err(),
+        "open_source refuses the link"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        io.read_bounded(&link, 64).is_err(),
+        "read_bounded refuses the link"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        io.read_tail(&link, 64).is_err(),
+        "read_tail refuses the link"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        open_regular_no_follow(td.path()).is_err(),
+        "a directory is refused"
+    );
+
+    let mut opened = io.open_source(&target).expect("the target opens");
+    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
+    opened.read_to_end(&mut bytes).expect("read the target");
+    assert_eq!(bytes, b"payload");
+}

+fn open_source_refuses_links_and_fifos_unix() {
+    use super::open_regular_no_follow;
+    use std::io::Read;
+    use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
+    let td = temp();

+    let link = td.path().join("link.bin");

+        open_regular_no_follow(&link).is_err(),
+        "the link is refused"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        io.open_source(&link).is_err(),
+        "open_source refuses the link"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        io.read_bounded(&link, 64).is_err(),
+        "read_bounded refuses the link"
+    );

+    let fifo = td.path().join("pipe");
+    let made = std::process::Command::new("/usr/bin/mkfifo")
+        .arg(&fifo)
+        .status()
+        .expect("mkfifo runs");
+    assert!(made.success(), "mkfifo made the FIFO");
+    let started = Instant::now();
+    assert!(io.open_source(&fifo).is_err(), "the FIFO is refused");
+    assert!(
+        io.read_bounded(&fifo, 64).is_err(),
+        "read_bounded refuses the FIFO"
+    );
+    assert!(
+        started.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(1),
+        "the FIFO refusals returned in {:?}, under 1 s",
+        started.elapsed()
+    );
+
+    let mut opened = io.open_source(&target).expect("the regular file opens");
+    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
+    opened.read_to_end(&mut bytes).expect("read the target");
+    assert_eq!(bytes, b"payload");
+}

+    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;

+    let run = td.path().join("run-0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef");

+    io.create_helper_folder(&folder)
+        .expect("create_helper_folder");
+    io.create_helper_folder(&folder).expect("present is Ok");
+    let mode = std::fs::symlink_metadata(&folder)
+        .expect("lstat the folder")
+        .mode();
+    assert_eq!(
+        mode & 0o022,
+        0,
+        "the helper folder is not group / other writable: {mode:o}"
+    );
+}

+#[cfg(unix)]
+#[test]
+fn wait_timeout_leaves_child_unix() {
+    use super::OsRunner;
+    use crate::seams::ProcessRunner;
+    use std::ffi::OsString;
+    use std::time::Duration;
+    let runner = OsRunner::new();
+    let argv: Vec<OsString> = ["/bin/sleep", "30"]
+        .into_iter()
+        .map(OsString::from)
+        .collect();
+    let id = runner.spawn(&argv).expect("spawn sleep");
+    assert_eq!(
+        runner.wait(id, Duration::from_millis(200)).expect("wait"),
+        None
+    );
+    assert_eq!(
+        runner.wait(id, Duration::ZERO).expect("second wait"),
+        None,
+        "the child was not killed"
+    );
+    let mut child = runner
+        .children
+        .lock()
+        .expect("the children map")
+        .remove(&id.0)
+        .expect("the child is still held");
+    assert_eq!(child.try_wait().expect("try_wait"), None, "still running");
+    child.kill().expect("kill the child");
+    child.wait().expect("reap the child");
+}

+//! The result codec (plan §4.1.5; FR-003, SI-11, SI-28).
+
+use crate::seams::WriteSync;
+use serde::Serialize;
+use std::io;
+
+/// The result file (FR-003); with `job_error` set, `ops` is empty (SI-11).
+#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize)]
+pub struct ResultFile {
+    pub result_version: u32,
+    pub job_sha256: String,
+    pub job_error: Option<&'static str>,
+    pub ops: Vec<OpEntry>,
+}
+
+/// One op's entry (FR-003).
+#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize)]
+pub struct OpEntry {
+    pub index: u32,
+    pub op: &'static str,
+    pub outcome: OpOutcome,
+    pub code: Option<&'static str>,
+    pub exit_code: Option<i32>,
+    pub seconds: f64,
+    pub log_tail: Vec<String>,
+}
+
+/// An op's outcome (FR-003).
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
+#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
+pub enum OpOutcome {
+    Done,
+    Locked,
+    Failed,
+    Refused,
+    NotRun,
+}
+
+/// FR-003: `log_tail` keeps at most this many lines.
+const TAIL_LINES: usize = 40;
+/// FR-003: each `log_tail` line holds at most this many bytes.
+const TAIL_LINE_BYTES: usize = 512;
+
+/// `serde_json::to_vec`, `write_all`, `sync_all` (FR-003).
+pub fn write(out: &mut dyn WriteSync, file: &ResultFile) -> io::Result<()> {
+    let bytes = serde_json::to_vec(file).map_err(io::Error::other)?;
+    out.write_all(&bytes)?;
+    out.sync_all()
+}
+
+/// The raw lines of `bytes`: split on `\n`, one trailing `\r` stripped; a final `\n` ends the last line and adds none.
+fn raw_lines(bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<&[u8]> {
+    if bytes.is_empty() {
+        return Vec::new();
+    }
+    let body = bytes.strip_suffix(b"\n").unwrap_or(bytes);
+    body.split(|b| *b == b'\n')
+        .map(|line| line.strip_suffix(b"\r").unwrap_or(line))
+        .collect()
+}
+
+/// Every line of `bytes` (split on `\n`, a trailing `\r` stripped), lossy UTF-8: `classify`'s input (SI-28).
+pub fn log_lines(bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<String> {
+    raw_lines(bytes)
+        .into_iter()
+        .map(|line| String::from_utf8_lossy(line).into_owned())
+        .collect()
+}
+
+/// FR-003: the last 40 lines (empty lines counted), each cut to at most 512 bytes at a char boundary, lossy UTF-8.
+pub fn log_tail(bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<String> {
+    let lines = raw_lines(bytes);
+    let first = lines.len().saturating_sub(TAIL_LINES);
+    lines[first..]
+        .iter()
+        .map(|line| {
+            let mut s = String::from_utf8_lossy(line).into_owned();
+            let mut end = s.len().min(TAIL_LINE_BYTES);
+            while !s.is_char_boundary(end) {
+                end -= 1;
+            }
+            s.truncate(end);
+            s
+        })
+        .collect()
+}
+

+#[path = "result_tests.rs"]
+mod tests;

+//! `result::tests` (owner T003; plan §7.1 SC-006 row): FR-003's file and the bounded log tail.
+
+use super::*;
+use std::io::Write;
+
+/// What a `WriteSync` was asked to do, in order.
+#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+enum Step {
+    Write(Vec<u8>),
+    Flush,
+    Sync,
+}
+
+#[derive(Default)]
+struct Recorder {
+    steps: Vec<Step>,
+}
+
+impl Write for Recorder {
+    fn write(&mut self, buf: &[u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
+        self.steps.push(Step::Write(buf.to_vec()));
+        Ok(buf.len())
+    }
+
+    fn flush(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
+        self.steps.push(Step::Flush);
+        Ok(())
+    }
+}
+
+impl WriteSync for Recorder {
+    fn sync_all(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
+        self.steps.push(Step::Sync);
+        Ok(())
+    }
+}
+
+/// `n` numbered lines, each ended by `\n`.
+fn numbered(n: usize) -> Vec<u8> {
+    (0..n)
+        .map(|i| format!("line {i}\n"))
+        .collect::<String>()
+        .into_bytes()
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn log_tail_bounds() {
+    // 45 lines keep the last 40, in order.
+    let tail = log_tail(&numbered(45));
+    assert_eq!(tail.len(), 40);
+    assert_eq!(tail.first().map(String::as_str), Some("line 5"));
+    assert_eq!(tail.last().map(String::as_str), Some("line 44"));
+    // The spec's row: 100 lines keep the last 40.
+    let tail = log_tail(&numbered(100));
+    assert_eq!(
+        tail,
+        (60..100).map(|i| format!("line {i}")).collect::<Vec<_>>()
+    );
+    // Fewer than 40 lines are kept whole; the last line needs no newline.
+    assert_eq!(log_tail(b"a\r\nb"), ["a", "b"]);
+
+    // 45 lines interleaved with empty lines: the empty lines count.
+    let interleaved: String = (0..45).map(|i| format!("line {i}\n\n")).collect();
+    let tail = log_tail(interleaved.as_bytes());
+    assert_eq!(tail.len(), 40);
+    let expected: Vec<String> = (25..45)
+        .flat_map(|i| [format!("line {i}"), String::new()])
+        .collect();
+    assert_eq!(tail, expected);
+
+    // A 600-byte line is cut to at most 512 bytes at a char boundary: 'a' then 300 two-byte chars puts byte 512
+    // inside a char, so the cut lands at 511.
+    let long = format!("a{}", "\u{e9}".repeat(300));
+    assert_eq!(long.len(), 601);
+    let tail = log_tail(long.as_bytes());
+    assert_eq!(tail.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(tail[0].len(), 511);
+    assert!(long.starts_with(&tail[0]));
+    // The spec's row: a 2000-byte ASCII line is cut to exactly 512 bytes.
+    let ascii = "x".repeat(2000);
+    assert_eq!(log_tail(ascii.as_bytes()), ["x".repeat(512)]);
+    // A line of exactly 512 bytes is kept whole.
+    assert_eq!(log_tail("y".repeat(512).as_bytes()), ["y".repeat(512)]);
+
+    // Invalid UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD.
+    assert_eq!(
+        log_tail(b"ok\n\xff\xfe bad\n"),
+        ["ok", "\u{fffd}\u{fffd} bad"]
+    );
+    // An empty input (an empty or missing log) is no lines.
+    assert_eq!(log_tail(b""), Vec::<String>::new());
+
+    // log_lines: every line, a trailing \r stripped, lossy UTF-8; a final newline adds no line.
+    let lines = log_lines(&numbered(45));
+    assert_eq!(lines.len(), 45);
+    assert_eq!(lines[0], "line 0");
+    assert_eq!(lines[44], "line 44");
+    assert_eq!(
+        log_lines(b"one\r\n\r\ntwo\r\nthree\r\r\n\xc3\n"),
+        ["one", "", "two", "three\r", "\u{fffd}"]
+    );
+    assert_eq!(log_lines(b""), Vec::<String>::new());
+    assert_eq!(log_lines(b"\n"), [""]);
+    // log_lines does not cut long lines: classify sees the whole line (SI-28).
+    assert_eq!(log_lines(ascii.as_bytes()), [ascii.as_str()]);
+
+    // write: FR-003's keys in order, then sync_all exactly once, after the bytes.
+    let file = ResultFile {
+        result_version: 1,
+        job_sha256: "ab".repeat(32),
+        job_error: None,
+        ops: vec![
+            OpEntry {
+                index: 0,
+                op: "run_inno_installer",
+                outcome: OpOutcome::Done,
+                code: None,
+                exit_code: Some(0),
+                seconds: 1.5,
+                log_tail: vec!["done".to_owned()],
+            },
+            OpEntry {
+                index: 1,
+                op: "run_inno_uninstaller",
+                outcome: OpOutcome::NotRun,
+                code: Some("E_HELPER_AFTER_TIMEOUT"),
+                exit_code: None,
+                seconds: 0.0,
+                log_tail: Vec::new(),
+            },
+        ],
+    };
+    let mut out = Recorder::default();
+    write(&mut out, &file).expect("the write succeeds");
+    let syncs = out.steps.iter().filter(|s| **s == Step::Sync).count();
+    assert_eq!(syncs, 1, "sync_all once: {:?}", out.steps);
+    assert_eq!(out.steps.last(), Some(&Step::Sync), "sync_all is last");
+    let bytes: Vec<u8> = out
+        .steps
+        .iter()
+        .filter_map(|s| match s {
+            Step::Write(b) => Some(b.clone()),
+            Step::Flush | Step::Sync => None,
+        })
+        .flatten()
+        .collect();
+    let expected = format!(
+        "{{\"result_version\":1,\"job_sha256\":\"{}\",\"job_error\":null,\"ops\":[\
+         {{\"index\":0,\"op\":\"run_inno_installer\",\"outcome\":\"done\",\"code\":null,\"exit_code\":0,\
+         \"seconds\":1.5,\"log_tail\":[\"done\"]}},\
+         {{\"index\":1,\"op\":\"run_inno_uninstaller\",\"outcome\":\"not_run\",\"code\":\"E_HELPER_AFTER_TIMEOUT\",\
+         \"exit_code\":null,\"seconds\":0.0,\"log_tail\":[]}}]}}",
+        "ab".repeat(32)
+    );
+    assert_eq!(String::from_utf8(bytes).expect("UTF-8"), expected);
+
+    // An exit-3 file (SI-11): job_error set, ops empty.
+    let refused = ResultFile {
+        result_version: 1,
+        job_sha256: "cd".repeat(32),
+        job_error: Some("E_HELPER_JOB_HASH"),
+        ops: Vec::new(),
+    };
+    let mut out = Recorder::default();
+    write(&mut out, &refused).expect("the write succeeds");
+    assert_eq!(out.steps.last(), Some(&Step::Sync));
+    let bytes: Vec<u8> = out
+        .steps
+        .iter()
+        .filter_map(|s| match s {
+            Step::Write(b) => Some(b.clone()),
+            Step::Flush | Step::Sync => None,
+        })
+        .flatten()
+        .collect();
+    assert_eq!(
+        String::from_utf8(bytes).expect("UTF-8"),
+        format!(
+            "{{\"result_version\":1,\"job_sha256\":\"{}\",\"job_error\":\"E_HELPER_JOB_HASH\",\"ops\":[]}}",
+            "cd".repeat(32)
+        )
+    );
+}
+
+/// A writer whose `write` fails: `write` reports it and never syncs.
+struct Failing {
+    synced: bool,
+}
+
+impl Write for Failing {
+    fn write(&mut self, _buf: &[u8]) -> io::Result<usize> {
+        Err(io::Error::from(io::ErrorKind::StorageFull))
+    }
+
+    fn flush(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
+        Ok(())
+    }
+}
+
+impl WriteSync for Failing {
+    fn sync_all(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
+        self.synced = true;
+        Ok(())
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn write_error_is_reported() {
+    let file = ResultFile {
+        result_version: 1,
+        job_sha256: "ab".repeat(32),
+        job_error: None,
+        ops: Vec::new(),
+    };
+    let mut out = Failing { synced: false };
+    let err = write(&mut out, &file).expect_err("the write fails");
+    assert_eq!(err.kind(), io::ErrorKind::StorageFull);
+    assert!(!out.synced, "no sync after a failed write");
+}
